```

## 3.1 GraphQL Contract Notes

| Topic | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Interface style | Strongly typed with `Atom` interface and `AtomResult` union |
| Analysis ingestion | Explicit mutation rather than hidden source adapter |
| Trust states | Included directly in `ValidationState` enum |
| Assembly | Query-driven but returns recipe-aware result |
| Cross-domain trace | Dedicated query families such as `traceRuleToCode` and `impactAnalysis` |

* * *

# 4. External Analysis Integration Spec v1

## Federated Analytical Producer Contract

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_external_analysis_integration_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`

## 4.1 Purpose

This specification defines how external analytical systems, especially linguistic-analysis systems, publish structured outputs into UAKS-v1 without collapsing the boundary between evidence and derivative interpretation.

The external system is a **parallel producer**, not a raw source adapter and not a canonical truth authority.

## 4.2 Core Design Rule

The integration model MUST be:

| Stage | Role |
| --- | --- |
| Publish | External system emits structured analytical envelope |
| Normalize | UAKS translation membrane maps foreign schema to local ontology |
| Ingest | Normalized analysis objects become governed atoms and relations |

## 4.3 Exchange Envelope

The external system MUST publish machine-readable result bundles.

### 4.3.1 Required Envelope Fields

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `analysis_run_id` | String | Unique run identifier |
| `analysis_type` | String | Discourse, semantic drift, syntax, rhetoric, etc. |
| `engine_name` | String | External system identity |
| `engine_version` | String | Version |
| `method` | String | Algorithm/model/process |
| `timestamp` | DateTime | Time produced |
| `source_scope` | Object | Corpus slice analyzed |
| `parameters` | Object | Run parameters |
| `findings` | Array | Analytical findings |
| `relations` | Array | Proposed cross-object edges |
| `ontology_version` | String | External ontology version if any |
| `schema_version` | String | Exchange schema version |

### 4.3.2 Finding Object Fields

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `finding_id` | String | Stable ID within run or across runs |
| `finding_kind` | String | observation, interpretation, metric, taxonomy_proposal, signature, drift |
| `finding_text` | String | Human-readable normalized statement |
| `confidence` | Float/String | Confidence score or category |
| `evidence_refs` | Array | Source spans or internal IDs |
| `tags` | Array | External tags |
| `supersedes` | String | Prior finding ID if relevant |
| `metrics` | Object | Numeric details if applicable |
| `cluster_id` | String | External grouping ID if applicable |
| `time_window_a` | Object | For drift or comparative runs |
| `time_window_b` | Object | For drift or comparative runs |
| `suggested_relations` | Array | Proposed edges to atoms/entities |

## 4.4 Source Scope Contract

The external system MUST define the analyzed corpus precisely enough for reproducibility.

| Scope Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `source_ids` | Source objects or external source identifiers |
| `conversation_ids` | Conversational units analyzed |
| `document_ids` | Documents analyzed |
| `project_scope` | Project boundary |
| `author_scope` | Author or actor filter |
| `time_window` | Temporal slice |
| `selection_filters` | Any additional filtering logic |

## 4.5 Normalization Membrane

The normalization membrane is mandatory. It MUST insulate UAKS from foreign assumptions.

### 4.5.1 Membrane Functions

| Function | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Identifier resolution | Map external references to internal source/atom/entity IDs |
| Ontology mapping | Convert external categories to local atom types |
| Trust assignment | Apply imported/machine-derived states |
| Evidence binding | Link findings to source spans or evidence atoms |
| Deduplication | Merge equivalent findings while preserving lineage |
| Provenance generation | Record analysis and transformation provenance |
| Relation generation | Convert suggested relations into typed graph edges |

### 4.5.2 Membrane Output Types

| External Finding Kind | Internal Type |
| --- | --- |
| observation | ObservationAtom |
| interpretation | InterpretationAtom |
| metric | MetricAtom |
| taxonomy_proposal | TaxonomyProposalAtom |
| signature | SignatureAtom |
| drift | DriftAtom |
| run_metadata | AnalysisRunAtom |

## 4.6 Provenance Requirements

Analytical imports require a three-layer provenance model.

### 4.6.1 Source Provenance

| Field | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| corpus scope | MUST be recorded |
| source references | MUST be recorded if available |
| time scope | SHOULD be recorded if relevant |

### 4.6.2 Analysis Provenance

| Field | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| engine name | MUST be recorded |
| engine version | SHOULD be recorded |
| method | MUST be recorded |
| parameters | SHOULD be recorded |
| ontology/dictionary version | SHOULD be recorded |
| timestamp | MUST be recorded |

### 4.6.3 Transformation Provenance

| Field | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| import job ID | MUST be recorded |
| normalization schema version | MUST be recorded |
| conflict-resolution logic | SHOULD be recorded |
| deduplication outcome | SHOULD be recorded |

## 4.7 Trust and Validation Policy

Imported analysis MUST NOT silently become canonical truth.

| State | Meaning | Assembly Eligibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IMPORTED | Raw accepted bundle | No normal assembly |
| MACHINE_DERIVED | Parsed and normalized | Exploratory only by default |
| REVIEWED | Human/trusted review completed | Conditional |
| ACCEPTED | Approved for normal assembly | Yes |
| EXPERIMENTAL | Research lane only | No standard assembly |
| DISPUTED | Challenged | No |
| DEPRECATED | Retired | No |

## 4.8 Relation Mapping

The integration layer SHOULD map useful external edges into the internal relation vocabulary.

| External Intent | Internal Relation |
| --- | --- |
| analyzed corpus | ANALYZES |
| aligns to concept | MAPS_TO |
| proposes category | PROPOSES_TAXONOMY_FOR |
| detects change | DETECTS_DRIFT_IN |
| structurally describes | CHARACTERIZES |
| flags re-evaluation | REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF |
| replaces prior analysis | IS_SUPERSEDED_BY |

## 4.9 Deduplication and Conflict Rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| EAI-DEDUP-01 | Equivalent findings MAY be merged if provenance multiplicity is preserved. |
| EAI-DEDUP-02 | Conflicting findings MUST remain separately representable. |
| EAI-DEDUP-03 | New findings that replace old findings SHOULD use supersession edges. |

## 4.10 Synchronous vs Asynchronous Integration

The systems SHOULD be operationally asynchronous and logically composable.

| Mode | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| External run execution | Asynchronous |
| Bundle publishing | Asynchronous |
| Ingestion into UAKS | Controlled pipeline |
| Query-time retrieval | Federated or pre-ingested |
| Assembly-time inclusion | Policy-gated |

## 4.11 Assembly Policy for Analysis Objects

Analysis objects MAY be included differently based on output mode.

| Output Mode | Analysis Inclusion Policy |
| --- | --- |
| Standard operational output | Only `ACCEPTED` analysis atoms |
| Research output | `ACCEPTED`, `REVIEWED`, optionally `EXPERIMENTAL` |
| Exploratory output | `MACHINE_DERIVED` allowed if explicitly requested |
| Compliance / high-trust output | Analysis lane excluded unless specifically approved |

## 4.12 Initial Minimal Import JSON Shape

JSON

```
{  "analysis_run_id": "run_20260311_001",  "analysis_type": "semantic_drift",  "engine_name": "external_linguistic_engine",  "engine_version": "1.2.0",  "method": "embedding_drift_plus_topic_alignment",  "timestamp": "2026-03-11T12:00:00Z",  "source_scope": {    "project_scope": "project_alpha",    "source_ids": ["src_001", "src_002"],    "time_window": {      "start": "2025-01-01T00:00:00Z",      "end": "2026-03-01T00:00:00Z"    }  },  "parameters": {    "cluster_count": 12,    "similarity_threshold": 0.82  },  "findings": [    {      "finding_id": "finding_001",      "finding_kind": "drift",      "finding_text": "The concept 'validation' shifted from schema conformance toward policy enforcement across the corpus.",      "confidence": 0.91,      "evidence_refs": ["src_001#L10-L20", "src_002#L33-L44"],      "tags": ["semantic_drift", "validation", "policy"],      "time_window_a": {        "start": "2025-01-01T00:00:00Z",        "end": "2025-06-30T23:59:59Z"      },      "time_window_b": {        "start": "2025-07-01T00:00:00Z",        "end": "2026-03-01T00:00:00Z"      },      "suggested_relations": [        {          "relation_type": "REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF",          "target_id": "atom_policy_123"        }      ]    }  ],  "relations": []}
```

## 4.13 Final Integration Judgment

The external analytical system is now formally modeled as:

| Role | Status |
| --- | --- |
| Raw source adapter | No |
| Canonical truth authority | No |
| Parallel analytical producer | Yes |
| First-class integration lane | Yes |

That is the correct abstraction boundary.

| 2 | Text Refinery Specification v1 |
| 3 | Code Refinery Specification v1 |

# Schema Pack v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

### Machine-Oriented Core Type System

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_schema_pack_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft implementation schema pack

This schema pack converts the ontology charter and API contract into an implementation-facing object model. It is written as a technology-neutral specification, with JSON-like field definitions and constraints. The intent is to provide a stable substrate that can later be rendered as JSON Schema, TypeScript types, GraphQL-backed resolvers, relational migrations, graph node/edge models, and validation contracts.

* * *

# 1. Schema Pack Scope

This pack defines the canonical structures for:

| Schema Group | Included |
| --- | --- |
| Base substrate | Atom, Entity, RelationEdge, SourceObject, ProvenanceRecord |
| Primary atomic domain | TextAtom, CodeAtom, EvidenceAtom, PolicyAtom, RecipeAtom, RelationAtom |
| Analytical derivative domain | AnalysisAtom, ObservationAtom, InterpretationAtom, MetricAtom, TaxonomyProposalAtom, SignatureAtom, DriftAtom, AnalysisRunAtom |
| Governance domain | ValidationEvent, TrustPolicyBinding |
| Assembly domain | Recipe, TemplateRef, AssemblyRequest, AssemblyResult |
| CAS domain | CASObject |
| Import domain | AnalysisImportEnvelope, AnalysisImportFinding |
| Utility domain | Span, TimeWindow, ScopeDescriptor, ConfidenceDescriptor |

* * *

# 3. Utility Schemas

## 3.1 Span

A span identifies a precise location within a source or code artifact.

JSON

```
{  "type": "object",  "name": "Span",  "required": ["locatorType", "start", "end"],  "properties": {    "locatorType": {      "type": "string",      "enum": ["LINE_COLUMN", "BYTE_OFFSET", "TOKEN_RANGE", "AST_PATH", "TIME_OFFSET"]    },    "start": {      "type": "object",      "required": ["value"],      "properties": {        "line": { "type": "integer" },        "column": { "type": "integer" },        "byte": { "type": "integer" },        "token": { "type": "integer" },        "value": { "type": "string" }      }    },    "end": {      "type": "object",      "required": ["value"],      "properties": {        "line": { "type": "integer" },        "column": { "type": "integer" },        "byte": { "type": "integer" },        "token": { "type": "integer" },        "value": { "type": "string" }      }    },    "displayText": { "type": "string" }  }}
```

## 3.2 TimeWindow

JSON

```
{  "type": "object",  "name": "TimeWindow",  "required": ["start", "end"],  "properties": {    "start": { "type": "string", "format": "date-time" },    "end": { "type": "string", "format": "date-time" },    "label": { "type": "string" }  }}
```

## 3.3 ConfidenceDescriptor

JSON

```
{  "type": "object",  "name": "ConfidenceDescriptor",  "required": ["score"],  "properties": {    "score": { "type": "number", "minimum": 0, "maximum": 1 },    "method": { "type": "string" },    "label": { "type": "string" }  }}
```

## 3.4 ScopeDescriptor

JSON

```

"type": "string" },    "canonicalPayloadRef": { "type": "string" },    "title": { "type": "string" },    "sourceRef": { "type": "string" },    "provenanceRefs": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "string" } },    "validationState": {      "type": "string",      "enum": [        "DRAFT",        "DISTILLED",        "REVIEWED",        "VERIFIED",        "DEPRECATED",        "DISPUTED",        "IMPORTED",        "MACHINE_DERIVED",        "ACCEPTED",        "EXPERIMENTAL"      ]    },    "confidence": { "$ref": "ConfidenceDescriptor" },    "taxonomyTags": { "type": "array", "items": { # 11. Invariants and Validation Rules

## 11.1 Global invariants

| Invariant ID | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| INV-001 | Every atom MUST have `atomId`, `atomType`, `atomClass`, `contentHash`, and `canonicalPayloadRef`. |
| INV-002 | Every verified or accepted atom MUST have at least one provenance reference. |
| INV-003 | `supersedes` and `supersededBy` MUST NOT both point to self. |
| INV-004 | `contentHash` MUST correspond to an existing CAS object for persisted payloads. |
| INV-005 | Every `RelationEdge.sourceId` and `RelationEdge.targetId` MUST resolve to existing IDs. |

## 11.2 Text invariants

| Invariant ID | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| TXT-001 | `TextAtom.canonicalStatement` MUST be non-empty. |
| TXT-002 | `TextAtom.sourceSpan` MUST exist. |
| TXT-003 | `TextAtom.entitiesMentioned` SHOULD resolve where applicable. |

## 11.3 Code invariants

| Invariant ID | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| CODE-001 | `CodeAtom.language`, `CodeAtom.astNodeType`, `CodeAtom.modulePath`, and `CodeAtom.astSpan` MUST exist. |
| CODE-002 | `CodeAtom.contentAddressRef` MUST resolve to a CAS object. |
| CODE-003 | `CodeAtom.codeKind` MUST be one of the defined code categories. |

## 11.4 Analysis invariants

| Invariant ID | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| AN-001 | Every imported analysis object MUST trace back to an `AnalysisRunAtom`. |
| AN-002 | Analytical objects in `ACCEPTED` state MUST have analysis provenance and transformation provenance. |
| AN-003 | `DriftAtom` MUST contain both `timeWindowA` and `timeWindowB`. |

* * *

# 13. Implementation Mapping Guidance

This schema pack is technology-neutral, but the intended mapping is straightforward.

| Target implementation | Mapping guidance |
| --- | --- |
| Graph database | Atoms and entities as nodes, RelationEdge as edges, provenance optionally as nodes |
| Relational database | Base tables per object family, enum tables optional, relation table normalized |
| CAS store | `canonicalPayloadRef` and `contentAddressRef` map to immutable object records |
| GraphQL | Types map directly to API contract already drafted |
| TypeScript | `AtomBase` becomes a base interface with discriminated unions |
| JSON Schema | Each object block can be converted into a strict schema file |

* * *

That is the first place where the abstract model becomes operational, because it defines how raw language is segmented, distilled, typed, deduplicated, and validated into `TextAtom`, `EvidenceAtom`, and `PolicyAtom`.

After that, the correct sequence is:

# Text Refinery Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_text_refinery_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

The Text Refinery converts raw language-bearing sources into governed atomic objects suitable for storage, retrieval, traceability, and assembly. It is the canonical upstream producer for `TextAtom`, `EvidenceAtom`, and `PolicyAtom`, and may also emit supporting entities, relations, and provenance records.

## 2. Inputs

| Input family | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| Conversational text | chats, transcripts, interviews, meetings |
| Documentary text | notes, specs, memos, policies, essays |
| Structured text | markdown, HTML, JSON-derived text fields |
| OCR-derived or imported text | PDF extraction, image-derived text, legacy corpus text |

## 3. Outputs

| Output type | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `TextAtom` | self-contained textual meaning |
| `EvidenceAtom` | source-backed supporting fragment |
| `PolicyAtom` | normative rule or governance statement |
| `Entity` | canonical referenced objects |
| `RelationEdge` | support, contradiction, refinement, derivation, use |
| `ProvenanceRecord` | refinery lineage |

## 4. Core refinery stages

| Stage | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TR-01 | Source normalization | convert input to normalized textual substrate |
| TR-02 | Structural segmentation | divide source into candidate units |
| TR-03 | Entity and cue extraction | detect actors, concepts, times, constraints, signals |
| TR-04 | Proposition decomposition | separate multi-claim units into simpler statements |
| TR-05 | Atomic distillation | rewrite into smallest independent units |
| TR-06 | Atom typing | classify as text, evidence, policy, and assign classes |
| TR-07 | Linking | create entity links, evidence links, parent-child links |
| TR-08 | Deduplication | merge near-equivalent atoms while preserving provenance |
| TR-09 | Validation | enforce atomicity, scope, provenance, and trust rules |
| TR-10 | Publication | persist atoms, relations, and provenance |

## 5. Stage requirements

### 5.1 TR-01 Source normalization

The refinery MUST normalize encodings, line endings, markup artifacts, and obvious extraction noise. It SHOULD preserve paragraph, heading, speaker-turn, and list structure where available.

### 5.2 TR-02 Structural segmentation

Segmentation SHOULD honor the native structure of the source.

| Source type | Preferred boundary signals |
| --- | --- |
| Transcript | speaker turn, timestamp, pause marker |
| Spec / memo | heading, paragraph, numbered clause |
| Notes | list item, paragraph, heading |
| Policy | clause, sentence, subclause |
| Documentation | heading, paragraph, example block |

### 5.3 TR-03 Entity and cue extraction

The refinery SHOULD extract:

| Cue family | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| Entities | people, organizations, projects, systems, concepts |
| Temporal cues | dates, ranges, sequence words |
| Normative cues | must, should, may, prohibited, required |
| Evidentiary cues | measured, observed, cited, quoted |
| Contrast cues | however, except, unless, unlike |
| Scope cues | for project X, in testing, under policy Y |

### 5.4 TR-04 Proposition decomposition

A segment containing multiple claims MUST be decomposed when those claims can stand independently.

Example:

| Input segment | Decomposed outputs |
| --- | --- |
| “The system stores recipes, and recipes update when atoms change.” | “The system stores recipes.” / “Recipes update when atoms change.” |

### 5.5 TR-05 Atomic distillation

A candidate atom passes atomic distillation only if it is independently comprehensible without hidden local context. Pronouns, omitted referents, and elliptical fragments SHOULD be resolved during distillation where possible.

### 5.6 TR-06 Atom typing

| Condition | Output |
| --- | --- |
| Normative or governance statement | `PolicyAtom` |
| Source-backed support fragment or measurement | `EvidenceAtom` |
| General self-contained textual meaning | `TextAtom` |

### 5.7 TR-07 Linking

The refinery SHOULD emit relations for:

| Relation | Trigger |
| --- | --- |
| `DERIVED_FROM` | every produced atom |
| `USES_ENTITY` | entity mention or resolution |
| `SUPPORTED_BY` | claim backed by evidence |
| `REFINES` | child atom is more precise than parent |
| `CONTRADICTS` | detected scoped conflict |
| `SUPERSEDES` | explicit replacement semantics in source |

### 5.8 TR-08 Deduplication

Near-equivalent atoms MAY be merged if meaning is materially the same and provenance is preserved as multiplicity. Contradictory or scope-divergent statements MUST NOT be collapsed.

### 5.9 TR-09 Validation

An atom MUST be rejected or downgraded if:

| Failure mode | Result |
| --- | --- |
| no source span | reject publication |
| unresolved opaque referents | re-distill or downgrade |
| mixed incompatible claims | decompose further |
| unclear scope on normative statement | hold for review |
| unsupported interpretive compression | mark as interpretation or downgrade |

### 5.10 TR-10 Publication

Published atoms MUST receive logical ID, content hash, CAS payload reference, provenance record, validation state, and created timestamps.

## 6. Atomicity tests

| Test ID | Test | Pass condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TAT-01 | Independence | statement stands alone |
| TAT-02 | Referential clarity | main referents explicit or linked |
| TAT-03 | Provenance trace | exact source span present |
| TAT-04 | Scope clarity | scope/temporal qualifiers present when needed |
| TAT-05 | Type fit | chosen atom type matches function |
| TAT-06 | Non-fusion | not improperly bundling multiple separable claims |

## 7. Text refinery decision heuristics

| Pattern | Preferred action |
| --- | --- |
| rule-like modal verbs | emit `PolicyAtom` |
| quotations and measurements | emit `EvidenceAtom` plus optional linked `TextAtom` |
| long descriptive paragraph | split into multiple `TextAtom`s |
| summary sentence derived from multiple spans | emit interpretive or summary atom only if marked and linked |

## 8. Minimal publication example

JSON

```
{  "atomId": "atom_text_014",  "atomType": "TEXT",  "atomClass": "FACT",  "contentHash": "sha256:abcd",  "canonicalPayloadRef": "cas_014",  "sourceRef": "src_memo_003",  "provenanceRefs": ["prov_014"],  "validationState": "DISTILLED",  "taxonomyTags": ["architecture", "assembly"],  "createdAt": "2026-03-11T16:00:00Z",  "updatedAt": "2026-03-11T16:00:00Z",  "status": "ACTIVE",  "textKind": "DOCUMENTATION_CLAUSE",  "canonicalStatement": "Documents are assembled projections rather than primary storage objects.",  "sourceSpan": {    "locatorType": "LINE_COLUMN",    "start": {"line": 12, "column": 1, "value": "12:1"},    "end": {"line": 12, "column": 73, "value": "12:73"}  },  "entitiesMentioned": []}
```

# Code Refinery Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_code_refinery_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

The Code Refinery converts technical sources into governed `CodeAtom`s, dependency edges, technical entities, provenance records, and traceability links to text and policy atoms. It MUST be structurally grounded and MUST NOT rely on naive text chunking as its primary method.

## 2. Inputs

| Input family | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| Source code | Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, Rust, etc. |
| Configuration | YAML, TOML, JSON, env templates |
| Schema definitions | SQL migrations, ORM models, interface schemas |
| Test code | unit tests, integration tests, assertions |
| Infrastructure | Dockerfiles, workflow files, IaC manifests |
| Shell and scripts | bash, zsh, PowerShell, CLI orchestration |

## 3. Outputs

| Output type | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `CodeAtom` | executable or technical logic unit |
| `Entity` | repos, modules, services, interfaces, schemas |
| `RelationEdge` | dependencies, tests, implementation links |
| `ProvenanceRecord` | parse and transformation lineage |

## 4. Core refinery stages

| Stage | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CR-01 | Source normalization | canonicalize file encoding and parser input |
| CR-02 | AST or structural parse | generate syntax/structure model |
| CR-03 | Symbol extraction | identify functions, methods, classes, modules, configs |
| CR-04 | Boundary determination | set atom spans |
| CR-05 | Signature and metadata extraction | inputs, outputs, language, runtime |
| CR-06 | Dependency analysis | build call/import/interface graph |
| CR-07 | Semantic tagging | side effects, complexity, role, domain |
| CR-08 | Cross-domain linking | map code to policy, docs, tests |
| CR-09 | Validation | ensure parse integrity and code atom validity |
| CR-10 | Publication | persist atoms, edges, provenance |

## 5. Stage requirements

### 5.1 CR-01 Source normalization

The refinery MUST preserve source-exact spans while normalizing encoding and line endings.

### 5.2 CR-02 AST or structural parse

Supported languages SHOULD use AST-aware parsers. Unsupported languages MAY use fallback structural parsing, but outputs SHOULD remain lower-trust until parser-grade support exists.

### 5.3 CR-03 Symbol extraction

The refinery MUST support at least these atom categories:

| Category | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| `FUNCTION` | standalone callable |
| `METHOD` | class-bound callable |
| `CLASS` | type definition |
| `MODULE` | file/package logical unit |
| `CONFIG` | config block |
| `QUERY` | SQL or query object |
| `SCRIPT_STEP` | shell/invocation step |
| `TEST_CASE` | test unit |
| `SCHEMA_UNIT` | schema or migration |
| `UI_COMPONENT` | component unit |
| `INFRA_UNIT` | deployment/infrastructure unit |

### 5.4 CR-04 Boundary determination

Atom boundaries SHOULD correspond to operationally meaningful spans, not arbitrary token windows. One file MAY produce many code atoms.

### 5.5 CR-05 Signature and metadata extraction

Where possible the refinery SHOULD extract:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `symbolName` | canonical symbol |
| `signature` | arguments, returns, modifiers |
| `language` | source language |
| `runtime` | interpreter/compiler/runtime |
| `astNodeType` | parser classification |
| `modulePath` | logical workspace path |

### 5.6 CR-06 Dependency analysis

The refinery SHOULD emit `DEPENDS_ON` and reverse dependency maps where robustly resolvable.

Dependency sources include:

| Dependency family | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| import/module | imports, requires, use statements |
| call graph | function or method calls |
| contract | interface or schema dependency |
| configuration | env or config requirement |
| data access | table/model/query dependency |

### 5.7 CR-07 Semantic tagging

The refinery SHOULD estimate:

| Tag | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| side effect profile | pure, read-only, write, network, filesystem, external |
| complexity score | static estimate |
| breaking-change risk | low/medium/high |
| technical role | parser, transport, validation, UI, persistence |

### 5.8 CR-08 Cross-domain linking

Where evidence exists, the refinery SHOULD permit links such as:

| Relation | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `IMPLEMENTS` | code realizes text/policy rule |
| `DOCUMENTS` | text describes code |
| `TESTS` | code/test validates code |
| `AFFECTS` | rule change impacts code |

### 5.9 CR-09 Validation

A `CodeAtom` MUST NOT be published as verified without:

| Requirement | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| parse integrity | span is structurally valid |
| source trace | exact file and span recorded |
| hash integrity | payload corresponds to CAS object |
| type integrity | atom category is valid |

### 5.10 CR-10 Publication

Published `CodeAtom`s MUST include content hash, CAS reference, source span, language, code kind, and timestamps.

## 6. Code refinery invariants

| Invariant ID | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| CRI-01 | one exact source span per published code atom |
| CRI-02 | one content-addressed payload reference per code atom |
| CRI-03 | supported-language code atoms SHOULD derive from parser output |
| CRI-04 | dependency edges MUST be directional |
| CRI-05 | code atoms MAY coexist across versions via supersession rather than overwrite |

## 7. Minimal publication example

JSON

```
{  "atomId": "atom_code_021",  "atomType": "CODE",  "atomClass": "PROCEDURE",  "contentHash": "sha256:efgh",  "canonicalPayloadRef": "cas_021",  "validationState": "DISTILLED",  "taxonomyTags": ["refinery", "text"],  "createdAt": "2026-03-11T16:10:00Z",  "updatedAt": "2026-03-11T16:10:00Z",  "status": "ACTIVE",  "codeKind": "FUNCTION",  "language": "python",  "runtime": "python3.12",  "astNodeType": "FunctionDef",  "symbolName": "segment_source_text",  "modulePath": "refinery/text/segment.py",  "astSpan": {    "locatorType": "LINE_COLUMN",    "start": {"line": 8, "column": 1, "value": "8:1"},    "end": {"line": 29, "column": 1, "value": "29:1"}  },  "sideEffectProfile": "PURE",  "contentAddressRef": "cas_code_021"}
```

# Graph Relation Catalog v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_graph_relation_catalog_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative catalog

## 1. Purpose

This catalog defines the canonical edge vocabulary for the graph substrate. Relations represent typed, directional semantics between atoms, entities, sources, and recipes.

## 2. General rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| GRC-01 | every edge MUST have a source and target |
| GRC-02 | every edge MUST have a declared relation type |
| GRC-03 | every verified edge SHOULD have provenance |
| GRC-04 | edge semantics MUST remain directional unless explicitly symmetric |
| GRC-05 | contradictory edges MAY coexist if scope or provenance differs |

## 3. Canonical relation catalog

| Relation | Source | Target | Directional | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `DERIVED_FROM` | Atom | SourceObject or Atom | Yes | output derives from prior object |
| `SUPPORTED_BY` | Atom | EvidenceAtom or Atom | Yes | claim is backed by support |
| `CONTRADICTS` | Atom | Atom | Usually symmetric | scoped conflict |
| `SUPERSEDES` | Atom | Atom | Yes | later state replaces earlier |
| `USES_ENTITY` | Atom | Entity | Yes | references or invokes entity |
| `ASSEMBLED_IN` | Atom | RecipeAtom | Yes | recipe membership |
| `REFINES` | Atom | Atom | Yes | more specific or more resolved form |
| `IMPLEMENTS` | CodeAtom | TextAtom or PolicyAtom | Yes | code realizes rule/statement |
| `DOCUMENTS` | TextAtom | CodeAtom | Yes | text describes code |
| `TESTS` | CodeAtom | CodeAtom | Yes | test validates target |
| `AFFECTS` | PolicyAtom or TextAtom | CodeAtom | Yes | change in rule impacts code |
| `EXPOSES_INTERFACE` | CodeAtom | Entity | Yes | code exposes contract/interface |
| `ANALYZES` | Analysis family | SourceObject or Atom | Yes | analysis studies target |
| `MAPS_TO` | Analysis family | Entity or Atom | Yes | external label aligned to local concept |
| `CHARACTERIZES` | Analysis family | Entity or corpus target | Yes | structural description |
| `DETECTS_DRIFT_IN` | DriftAtom | Entity or Atom | Yes | temporal change detected |
| `PROPOSES_TAXONOMY_FOR` | TaxonomyProposalAtom | corpus/entity/concept set | Yes | taxonomy suggestion |
| `REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF` | Analysis family | Atom | Yes | flags re-evaluation |
| `IS_SUPERSEDED_BY` | Analysis family | Analysis family | Yes | analytical successor |
| `DEPENDS_ON` | CodeAtom | CodeAtom or Entity | Yes | operational dependency |

## 4. Edge qualifiers

An edge MAY carry qualifiers.

| Qualifier | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| confidence | strength or trust score |
| scope | project/time/jurisdiction scope |
| provenanceRef | lineage record |
| effectiveFrom / effectiveTo | validity window |
| rationale | explanatory note |

## 5. Recommended inverses

Some inverses MAY be materialized for convenience.

| Primary edge | Optional inverse |
| --- | --- |
| `SUPERSEDES` | `IS_SUPERSEDED_BY` |
| `DEPENDS_ON` | `DEPENDENT_OF` as derived view only |
| `IMPLEMENTS` | `IMPLEMENTED_BY` as derived view only |
| `TESTS` | `TESTED_BY` as derived view only |

The canonical stored edge SHOULD remain the primary direction unless operational needs justify both.

## 6. High-value relation patterns

### 6.1 Rule to code trace

`PolicyAtom` → `AFFECTS` → `CodeAtom`  
`CodeAtom` → `IMPLEMENTS` → `PolicyAtom`

### 6.2 Claim support chain

`TextAtom` → `SUPPORTED_BY` → `EvidenceAtom`

### 6.3 Analysis-triggered maintenance

`DriftAtom` → `REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF` → `TextAtom`  
`TextAtom` → `AFFECTS` → `CodeAtom`

### 6.4 Assembly trace

`Atom` → `ASSEMBLED_IN` → `RecipeAtom`

# Validation and Trust Policy v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_validation_trust_policy_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative policy

## 1. Purpose

This policy governs lifecycle states, review thresholds, trust gradients, and assembly eligibility for all atom families.

## 2. Core validation states

| State | Meaning | Standard assembly eligibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `DRAFT` | created but not conformance-checked | No |
| `DISTILLED` | machine-produced and schema-valid | No |
| `REVIEWED` | human or trusted review applied | Conditional |
| `VERIFIED` | approved for normal assembly | Yes |
| `DEPRECATED` | retained, not active | No |
| `DISPUTED` | challenged or conflicting | No |

## 3. Analysis trust states

| State | Meaning | Standard assembly eligibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IMPORTED` | raw accepted external import | No |
| `MACHINE_DERIVED` | normalized machine analysis | No by default |
| `ACCEPTED` | approved for standard use | Yes |
| `EXPERIMENTAL` | exploratory research lane only | No by default |

## 4. Promotion rules

| From | To | Minimum requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `DRAFT` | `DISTILLED` | schema-valid and provenance present |
| `DISTILLED` | `REVIEWED` | reviewer check or trusted adjudicator |
| `REVIEWED` | `VERIFIED` | passes domain-specific trust threshold |
| `IMPORTED` | `MACHINE_DERIVED` | normalization membrane completed |
| `MACHINE_DERIVED` | `REVIEWED` | review performed |
| `REVIEWED` | `ACCEPTED` | approved for standard analytical inclusion |

## 5. Mandatory review cases

The following SHOULD require human or high-trust review before standard assembly:

| Atom family | Trigger |
| --- | --- |
| `PolicyAtom` | operational, legal, or governance consequence |
| `CodeAtom` | external side effects, high breaking-change risk |
| `InterpretationAtom` | inferential claims influencing decisions |
| `TaxonomyProposalAtom` | ontology-changing suggestion |
| `DriftAtom` | triggers taxonomy or policy review |
| any atom | contradiction or low-confidence ambiguity |

## 6. Assembly inclusion policy

| Output mode | Allowed states |
| --- | --- |
| Standard operational output | `VERIFIED`, `ACCEPTED` |
| Research output | `REVIEWED`, `VERIFIED`, `ACCEPTED`, optional `EXPERIMENTAL` |
| Exploratory output | may include `DISTILLED` and `MACHINE_DERIVED` if explicitly requested |
| High-trust/compliance output | `VERIFIED` only unless policy exception exists |

## 7. Dispute handling

A disputed atom MUST remain traceable, MUST retain provenance, and SHOULD remain queryable in forensic contexts while excluded from normal assembly.

## 8. Supersession handling

Superseded atoms MUST NOT be deleted merely because newer atoms exist. They SHOULD remain accessible for history, lineage, and comparison.

# Assembly Engine Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_assembly_engine_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

The Assembly Engine converts governed atoms into human-facing outputs or saved recipes. It does not originate truth. It selects, sequences, and renders truth-bearing substrate objects subject to trust, scope, and template rules.

## 2. Inputs

| Input | Description |
| --- | --- |
| query | user or system request |
| atom set | explicit list of atoms |
| recipe | prior assembly plan |
| template | output mold |
| resolution level | micro to deep |
| policy | trust and inclusion constraints |

## 3. Outputs

| Output | Description |
| --- | --- |
| rendered text | assembled human-readable output |
| recipe | reusable ordered atom plan |
| provenance refs | lineage exposure |
| warnings | ambiguity, missing support, low-trust inclusion |

## 4. Assembly stages

| Stage | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AE-01 | scope resolution | determine topic, audience, trust mode, depth |
| AE-02 | candidate retrieval | fetch atoms through hybrid retrieval |
| AE-03 | policy filtering | exclude ineligible states |
| AE-04 | structural grouping | group atoms by role and template needs |
| AE-05 | sequencing | order atoms logically |
| AE-06 | connective synthesis | generate transitions and framing |
| AE-07 | provenance packaging | attach source references if needed |
| AE-08 | recipe persistence | save reusable assembly plan |

## 5. Resolution model

| Resolution | Inclusion logic |
| --- | --- |
| `MICRO` | highest-centrality core atoms only |
| `COMPACT` | core atoms plus minimal support |
| `STANDARD` | balanced claim, support, and context |
| `EXTENDED` | detailed support and operational structure |
| `DEEP` | evidence lattice, edge cases, contradictions, and lineage |

## 6. Template classes

| Template class | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Summary | concise overview |
| Technical Spec | formal structured explanation |
| Trace Report | rule-code or source-claim lineage |
| Impact Report | downstream effect analysis |
| Decision Memo | rationale, options, recommendation |
| Research Synthesis | multi-layer evidence-rich exposition |

## 7. Engine rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| AE-R-01 | the engine MUST honor validation/trust policy |
| AE-R-02 | the engine MUST NOT present unsupported generated claims as substrate truth |
| AE-R-03 | connective prose MAY be generated |
| AE-R-04 | recipe capture SHOULD store atom order, template, and resolution |
| AE-R-05 | contradiction-aware outputs SHOULD surface scoped conflicts rather than flatten them |

## 8. Minimal recipe object

JSON

```
{  "recipeId": "recipe_101",  "recipeType": "technical_spec",  "resolutionLevel": "STANDARD",  "atomSequence": ["atom_text_014", "atom_policy_005", "atom_code_021"],  "templateRef": "template_technical_spec_v1",  "generationRules": {    "includeProvenance": true,    "includeAnalysis": false  },  "createdAt": "2026-03-11T16:30:00Z"}
```

## 9. Minimal assembly result

JSON

```
{  "resultId": "assembly_101",  "recipeRef": "recipe_101",  "renderedText": "The system stores knowledge as atoms and renders documents as assemblies. Policy logic is enforced by linked code paths.",  "atomIdsUsed": ["atom_text_014", "atom_policy_005", "atom_code_021"],  "provenanceRefs": ["prov_014", "prov_005", "prov_021"],  "warnings": []}
```

## Q: 
Proceed

## A: 
# Retrieval Scoring Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_retrieval_scoring_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

This specification defines how UAKS-v1 retrieves candidate atoms, entities, relations, sources, and recipes from the substrate. Retrieval in UAKS-v1 is hybrid by design. It combines lexical precision, semantic similarity, graph structure, provenance quality, temporal validity, trust state, and assembly fitness.

The retrieval layer exists to answer one question correctly:

**Which governed objects should be considered for this request, in what priority order, and under what trust constraints?**

## 2. Retrieval thesis

UAKS-v1 retrieval MUST NOT be reduced to any one of the following alone:

| Single-mode strategy | Why insufficient |
| --- | --- |
| Full-text search only | brittle to paraphrase, synonymy, and concept drift |
| Vector similarity only | weak on explicit logic, exact identifiers, and trust constraints |
| Graph traversal only | weak on latent semantic similarity and fuzzy recall |
| Latest-first only | weak on foundational centrality and long-lived axioms |
| Highest-confidence only | can suppress useful but novel or scoped findings |

The correct model is a weighted hybrid retrieval system with policy-aware filtering.

## 3. Retrieval objects

The retrieval layer MAY return the following object families:

| Object family | Retrieval role |
| --- | --- |
| `TextAtom` | primary textual knowledge |
| `CodeAtom` | executable or technical logic |
| `EvidenceAtom` | support and grounding |
| `PolicyAtom` | governance and normative rules |
| `AnalysisAtom` family | derivative analytical findings |
| `Entity` | concept and object anchors |
| `RelationEdge` | structural context |
| `RecipeAtom` / `Recipe` | prior useful assembly patterns |
| `SourceObject` | forensic source anchor |

## 4. Retrieval stages

| Stage | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RS-01 | Query normalization | interpret query, constraints, mode, and scope |
| RS-02 | Candidate generation | collect broad candidate pool from hybrid channels |
| RS-03 | Policy gating | remove ineligible objects based on trust and access |
| RS-04 | Scoring | compute final ranking scores |
| RS-05 | Diversification | avoid over-concentration in redundant near-duplicates |
| RS-06 | Expansion | pull support, parents, children, or dependencies if requested |
| RS-07 | Final selection | emit ranked set for assembly or inspection |

## 5. Query normalization

The retrieval system MUST normalize the incoming request into a formal query context.

### 5.1 QueryContext fields

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `queryText` | natural-language or structured query |
| `requestedObjectTypes` | atom families or objects requested |
| `resolutionLevel` | micro, compact, standard, extended, deep |
| `outputMode` | operational, research, exploratory, high-trust |
| `scopeDescriptor` | project/repository/entity/time constraints |
| `includeAnalysis` | whether analysis lane is eligible |
| `includeExperimental` | whether experimental objects may appear |
| `traceMode` | whether lineage/impact/trace relations are primary |
| `userSpecifiedIds` | explicit object anchors if any |

### 5.2 Query intent categories

| Intent | Typical use |
| --- | --- |
| fact retrieval | find direct governed claims |
| policy retrieval | find rules and constraints |
| code retrieval | find logic units and dependencies |
| trace retrieval | rule-to-code, source-to-claim, claim-to-evidence |
| impact retrieval | what changes if X changes |
| ontology retrieval | find entities, concepts, taxonomy |
| research synthesis | broad multi-source candidate pool |
| exploratory analysis | include machine-derived analytical objects |

## 6. Candidate generation channels

The system MUST support multiple candidate generation channels.

### 6.1 Lexical channel

Uses full-text or token-index search for exact or near-exact terms, identifiers, symbol names, tag matches, and constrained spans.

Best for:

| Best-case lexical targets |
| --- |
| exact terminology |
| code symbol names |
| policy phrases |
| quoted text |
| tags |
| identifiers |

### 6.2 Semantic channel

Uses embedding-space similarity against atoms, entity descriptions, code summaries, and normalized analytical findings.

Best for:

| Best-case semantic targets |
| --- |
| paraphrase |
| conceptual similarity |
| cross-domain topic recall |
| latent theme match |
| synonym and drift-tolerant discovery |

### 6.3 Graph channel

Uses structural traversal from anchored atoms or entities.

Best for:

| Best-case graph targets |
| --- |
| dependency lookup |
| support chains |
| contradiction chains |
| supersession chains |
| rule-to-code mapping |
| analysis-triggered review paths |

### 6.4 Recipe prior channel

Uses prior recipe usage and successful assembly patterns to seed likely candidates.

Best for:

| Best-case recipe targets |
| --- |
| recurring report structures |
| known documentation bundles |
| stable technical explanations |
| repeated trace workflows |

### 6.5 Recency and activity channel

Uses time-aware boosting from creation, validation, supersession, or recent successful use.

Best for:

| Best-case time-sensitive targets |
| --- |
| current policy |
| latest accepted analysis |
| recent code state |
| recent ontology changes |

## 7. Hard filters before scoring

The following SHOULD be applied before final ranking when relevant.

| Filter class | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| access control | remove unauthorized objects |
| validation threshold | exclude states not permitted by output mode |
| scope filter | restrict by project/repository/time/entity |
| type filter | restrict to requested object families |
| supersession policy | suppress superseded objects unless historical mode requested |
| analysis policy | exclude analytical lane if not requested or not allowed |

## 8. Core scoring model

The retrieval score for an object `o` under query context `q` SHOULD be modeled as:

$$
R(o,q) = w_lL(o,q) + w_sS(o,q) + w_gG(o,q) + w_tT(o,q) + w_vV(o,q) + w_cC(o) + w_uU(o) + w_aA(o,q) - w_dD(o,q)
$$

Where:

| Component | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| $L(o,q)$ | lexical relevance |
| $S(o,q)$ | semantic similarity |
| $G(o,q)$ | graph relevance |
| $T(o,q)$ | temporal relevance |
| $V(o,q)$ | validation/trust fit |
| $C(o)$ | structural centrality |
| $U(o)$ | utility from prior successful use |
| $A(o,q)$ | assembly fitness for the requested template/resolution |
| $D(o,q)$ | penalties for staleness, contradiction mismatch, redundancy, or scope mismatch |

## 9. Score component definitions

### 9.1 Lexical relevance $L$

Lexical relevance SHOULD combine term overlap, exact identifier hits, field-specific matches, and tag matches.

$$
L(o,q) = \alpha_1 \cdot exact_match + \alpha_2 \cdot bm25 + \alpha_3 \cdot tag_match + \alpha_4 \cdot identifier_match
$$

Lexical relevance SHOULD receive extra weight for:

| Case | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| code symbol match | often exact-intent |
| policy clause phrase match | precision critical |
| explicit atom or entity name | user intent likely narrow |
| quoted span | likely exact retrieval desired |

### 9.2 Semantic similarity $S$

Semantic similarity SHOULD be computed using cosine or equivalent similarity over embeddings of the query and candidate objects.

It SHOULD be available for:

| Object family | Embedding basis |
| --- | --- |
| `TextAtom` | canonical statement |
| `PolicyAtom` | policy statement |
| `EvidenceAtom` | normalized evidence text |
| `CodeAtom` | code summary, docstring, symbol context, linked descriptions |
| `AnalysisAtom` family | finding text |

### 9.3 Graph relevance $G$

Graph relevance SHOULD reflect how structurally close or important an object is relative to query anchors.

Possible subcomponents:

| Graph subcomponent | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| path proximity | shortest or weighted path from anchored objects |
| relation-type relevance | preferred edge types by query intent |
| dependency reachability | downstream or upstream relevance |
| support depth | closeness to evidence or claim anchors |
| supersession priority | latest valid object in a lineage |

### 9.4 Temporal relevance $T$

Temporal relevance MUST respect effective validity and SHOULD boost recency only where the query or object type warrants it.

$$
T(o,q) = \beta_1 \cdot current_validity + \beta_2 \cdot recency_fit + \beta_3 \cdot time_window_overlap
$$

Temporal weight SHOULD be high for policy, code state, and analysis findings, and lower for axioms or foundational concepts.

### 9.5 Validation/trust fit $V$

Validation fit measures whether the object’s trust state is appropriate to the output mode.

| Output mode | Preferred states |
| --- | --- |
| standard operational | `VERIFIED`, `ACCEPTED` |
| research | `REVIEWED`, `VERIFIED`, `ACCEPTED` |
| exploratory | may include `DISTILLED`, `MACHINE_DERIVED`, `EXPERIMENTAL` |
| high-trust | `VERIFIED` only unless exception exists |

Objects outside allowed states SHOULD receive infinite negative gating or severe penalty rather than minor downgrade.

### 9.6 Centrality $C$

Centrality reflects how foundational an object is in the substrate.

Candidate methods:

| Centrality method | Use |
| --- | --- |
| in-degree / out-degree | rough importance |
| eigenvector centrality | hub-like importance |
| dependency centrality | technical criticality |
| support centrality | evidentiary structural importance |

Centrality SHOULD be used carefully so that foundational hubs do not swamp query-specific relevance.

### 9.7 Utility $U$

Utility reflects how often an object has been used successfully in prior assemblies or workflows.

Candidate factors:

| Utility factor | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| recipe frequency | used in successful recipes |
| selection persistence | retained through review |
| user acceptance | historically useful outputs |
| low-revision stability | fewer later corrections |

### 9.8 Assembly fitness $A$

Assembly fitness scores whether an object fits the requested template and resolution.

Example:

| Template / resolution | Preferred candidates |
| --- | --- |
| summary / micro | central, high-density atoms |
| technical spec / extended | procedure, dependency, interface, policy, code |
| trace report | support, derived_from, implements, tests |
| research synthesis / deep | evidence, contradictions, analysis lane, provenance-rich objects |

### 9.9 Penalty term $D$

Penalty SHOULD combine negative factors.

| Penalty source | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| redundancy | near-duplicate candidate already selected |
| stale state | superseded or out-of-window object |
| scope mismatch | wrong project/time/jurisdiction |
| trust mismatch | insufficient validation for output mode |
| contradiction mismatch | candidate conflicts with required stance and lacks surfacing policy |

## 10. Default weight profiles

The system SHOULD support mode-specific weighting profiles.

### 10.1 General balanced profile

| Component | Default weight |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | 0.18 |
| semantic | 0.22 |
| graph | 0.18 |
| temporal | 0.08 |
| validation | 0.14 |
| centrality | 0.07 |
| utility | 0.05 |
| assembly fitness | 0.08 |

### 10.2 Policy retrieval profile

| Component | Default weight |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | 0.24 |
| semantic | 0.16 |
| graph | 0.14 |
| temporal | 0.10 |
| validation | 0.18 |
| centrality | 0.05 |
| utility | 0.03 |
| assembly fitness | 0.10 |

### 10.3 Code retrieval profile

| Component | Default weight |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | 0.20 |
| semantic | 0.12 |
| graph | 0.26 |
| temporal | 0.08 |
| validation | 0.12 |
| centrality | 0.08 |
| utility | 0.04 |
| assembly fitness | 0.10 |

### 10.4 Trace and impact profile

| Component | Default weight |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | 0.10 |
| semantic | 0.08 |
| graph | 0.38 |
| temporal | 0.10 |
| validation | 0.12 |
| centrality | 0.06 |
| utility | 0.04 |
| assembly fitness | 0.12 |

### 10.5 Research deep profile

| Component | Default weight |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | 0.12 |
| semantic | 0.22 |
| graph | 0.18 |
| temporal | 0.08 |
| validation | 0.10 |
| centrality | 0.06 |
| utility | 0.04 |
| assembly fitness | 0.20 |

These values are initial defaults only and SHOULD be tuned against the evaluation harness.

## 11. Type-specific scoring adjustments

### 11.1 TextAtom adjustments

`TextAtom` scoring SHOULD emphasize semantic and lexical fit, plus support availability.

Boost conditions:

| Condition | Boost |
| --- | --- |
| exact policy phrase or concept match | high |
| linked supporting evidence present | medium |
| directly refined from requested parent | medium |
| active and not superseded | medium |

### 11.2 CodeAtom adjustments

`CodeAtom` scoring SHOULD emphasize graph relevance, identifier match, dependency location, and linked policy/docs.

Boost conditions:

| Condition | Boost |
| --- | --- |
| symbol or module name exact match | high |
| direct dependency path from anchored code | high |
| linked `IMPLEMENTS` relation to relevant policy | medium-high |
| linked `TESTS` coverage | medium |

### 11.3 EvidenceAtom adjustments

`EvidenceAtom` SHOULD be weakly retrieved on its own unless the query requests support, audit, compliance, or deep explanation.

### 11.4 AnalysisAtom family adjustments

Analytical lane objects MUST obey trust policy gating. When included, they SHOULD score strongly for research, exploratory, drift, ontology, and discourse-oriented queries.

Boost conditions:

| Condition | Boost |
| --- | --- |
| accepted/reviewed state | medium-high |
| recent relevant analysis run | medium |
| direct `REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF` or `DETECTS_DRIFT_IN` link | high for maintenance queries |
| explicit include-analysis flag | high |

## 12. Diversification policy

The retrieval layer SHOULD avoid returning ten near-identical objects when three representative ones plus support objects would serve better.

Diversification MAY use:

| Method | Function |
| --- | --- |
| max marginal relevance | balance relevance and novelty |
| lineage collapsing | suppress superseded siblings |
| family quotas | avoid single-family dominance |
| source spread | represent multiple sources where useful |

Diversification SHOULD be relaxed for forensic or exact trace modes.

## 13. Expansion policy

After ranking the top core candidates, the system MAY pull structured expansion objects.

### 13.1 Expansion modes

| Expansion mode | Pulled objects |
| --- | --- |
| support expansion | evidence and supporting claims |
| dependency expansion | upstream/downstream code dependencies |
| parent-child expansion | refined or abstracted neighbors |
| provenance expansion | source and transformation records |
| contradiction expansion | scoped competing objects |
| recipe expansion | prior assembly neighbors |

Expansion MUST remain policy-aware and SHOULD preserve clear distinction between primary hits and context expansions.

## 14. Historical and supersession handling

### 14.1 Default behavior

Default retrieval SHOULD prefer active, non-superseded objects.

### 14.2 Historical mode

When the query requests history, comparison, drift, or lineage, superseded objects MUST remain retrievable and SHOULD be grouped by supersession chain.

### 14.3 Lineage grouping

The UI or assembly layer SHOULD be able to present:

| Grouping | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| current state | preferred active object |
| historical predecessors | prior superseded objects |
| successor chain | later replacements |
| scoped divergence | valid alternatives under different scopes |

## 15. Analytical lane inclusion policy

The retrieval layer MUST treat the analytical lane as derivative, policy-gated material.

### 15.1 Inclusion rules

| Output mode | Analytical inclusion |
| --- | --- |
| standard operational | `ACCEPTED` only, if relevant |
| research | `REVIEWED` and `ACCEPTED`, optionally `EXPERIMENTAL` |
| exploratory | may include `MACHINE_DERIVED` and `EXPERIMENTAL` if explicitly requested |
| high-trust | exclude unless explicitly authorized |

### 15.2 Rank dampening

Even when eligible, analytical objects SHOULD receive dampened ranking against direct primary substrate objects unless the query intent clearly favors derivative analysis.

## 16. Example scoring interpretation

Assume query: “Which code implements the validation policy for imported analysis bundles?”

Likely score priorities:

| Component | Importance |
| --- | --- |
| lexical | high for “validation”, “analysis bundles”, symbol names |
| graph | very high for `IMPLEMENTS`, `AFFECTS`, `DEPENDS_ON` |
| semantic | medium for paraphrased technical matches |
| temporal | medium if policies recently changed |
| validation | high, because implementation answers should be trustworthy |
| assembly fitness | high for trace report or technical spec mode |

Top likely objects:

| Rank band | Object type |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | `PolicyAtom` about validation policy |
| 2 | linked `CodeAtom` via `IMPLEMENTS` |
| 3 | linked `CodeAtom` test cases via `TESTS` |
| 4 | supporting `TextAtom` documentation |
| 5 | accepted `AnalysisAtom` only if query explicitly asks about analysis behavior |

## 17. Retrieval output contract

The retrieval layer SHOULD emit structured results, not only ranked IDs.

### 17.1 RetrievalResult schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `objectId` | retrieved object |
| `objectType` | object family |
| `finalScore` | combined score |
| `scoreBreakdown` | per-component contributions |
| `rank` | output rank |
| `matchReasons` | human-readable reasons |
| `expansionRole` | primary, support, dependency, provenance, contradiction |
| `warnings` | trust, staleness, ambiguity flags |

## 18. Evaluation hooks

The retrieval system MUST be measurable.

### 18.1 Primary evaluation measures

| Measure | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| precision@k | relevance of top results |
| recall@k | coverage of gold objects |
| mrr | mean reciprocal rank |
| support coverage | whether support/evidence included when needed |
| trust compliance | whether disallowed states are excluded |
| lineage correctness | whether current vs historical state is handled correctly |
| cross-domain trace accuracy | correctness of rule-to-code and analysis-to-source paths |

## 19. Required tuning artifacts

This retrieval specification depends on these companion artifacts:

| Companion artifact | Role |
| --- | --- |
| benchmark corpus definition | ground truth input set |
| gold-standard atom set | expected retrieval targets |
| query pattern library | canonical test queries |
| evaluation harness | automated scoring |
| validation/trust policy | gating rules |
| graph relation catalog | graph path semantics |

## 20. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 retrieval implementation MUST:

| Requirement | Status |
| --- | --- |
| support lexical retrieval | mandatory |
| support semantic retrieval | mandatory |
| support graph-aware retrieval | mandatory |
| apply trust-aware policy gating | mandatory |
| respect supersession and historical mode | mandatory |
| support type-specific weighting | mandatory |
| produce explainable ranked results | strongly recommended |
| support expansion modes | strongly recommended |

# Deduplication and Merge Policy v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_dedup_merge_policy_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative policy

## 1. Purpose

This policy defines when objects in the substrate may be merged, coalesced, clustered, or explicitly kept separate. The goal is to prevent redundancy bloom without collapsing meaningful distinctions in scope, provenance, trust, or semantics.

## 2. Deduplication thesis

UAKS-v1 MUST prefer **meaning-preserving consolidation** over raw object-count growth, but MUST NOT erase distinctions that matter to truth, scope, or auditability.

The system therefore distinguishes:

| Condition | Action |
| --- | --- |
| exact duplicate state | consolidate aggressively |
| semantic duplicate with same scope | merge with provenance multiplicity |
| near-duplicate with meaningful scope difference | keep distinct, relate |
| contradiction or contested meaning | keep distinct, mark conflict |
| supersession | keep both, link lineage |

## 3. Duplicate categories

| Category | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| exact duplicate | same canonical payload, same effective scope, same type |
| semantic duplicate | materially same meaning after normalization |
| structural duplicate | same relation or entity binding repeated |
| scoped variant | same core meaning but different project/time/jurisdiction/speaker scope |
| contradictory variant | incompatible or opposing meaning |
| superseded variant | older state replaced by later state |
| analytical echo | derivative analysis restating already stored accepted finding |

## 4. Merge eligibility rules

### 4.1 Exact duplicates

Exact duplicates SHOULD be merged into a single active object with preserved provenance multiplicity.

### 4.2 Semantic duplicates

Semantic duplicates MAY be merged only if all of the following hold:

| Requirement | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| same object family | no cross-family flattening |
| materially equivalent meaning | no loss of meaning |
| same effective scope | same domain/time/project/jurisdiction |
| compatible trust state | no unsafe upgrade by merge |
| provenance preserved | all sources retained |

### 4.3 Non-merge cases

Objects MUST NOT be merged when any of the following apply:

| Non-merge trigger | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| different scopes | truth differs by context |
| contradiction exists | conflict must remain visible |
| different epistemic tier | evidence vs interpretation vs analysis must remain distinct |
| supersession relation exists | lineage must remain explicit |
| different atom family with only superficial lexical similarity | ontology boundary preservation |

## 5. Canonical merge actions

| Action | Use |
| --- | --- |
| collapse-to-one | exact duplicates |
| merge-with-provenance | semantic duplicates |
| relate-as-refines | one is more precise |
| relate-as-supersedes | newer state replaces older |
| relate-as-contradicts | conflicting claims |
| cluster-only | similar objects grouped for retrieval but not merged |

## 6. Family-specific rules

### 6.1 TextAtom

`TextAtom`s MAY be merged when normalized meaning is materially identical and scope is aligned.

They MUST NOT be merged across:

| Boundary | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| project scope | context may differ |
| temporal state | policy or meaning may have changed |
| normative vs descriptive role | role changes truth-function |

### 6.2 EvidenceAtom

`EvidenceAtom`s SHOULD usually remain distinct unless they are byte-for-byte or span-for-span identical. Multiple independent evidence spans that support the same claim are better stored as multiple evidence atoms linked to a common claim.

### 6.3 PolicyAtom

`PolicyAtom`s SHOULD be merged conservatively. Even subtle wording changes MAY carry normative consequences. If two policies differ in modal force, exception structure, or applicability, they MUST remain distinct.

### 6.4 CodeAtom

`CodeAtom`s MUST NOT be merged merely because they are similar in behavior. Exact payload identity and stable content-addressed equivalence are required for hard merge. Otherwise they MAY be clustered as similar implementations or linked through refinement or supersession.

### 6.5 AnalysisAtom family

Analytical objects SHOULD be merged only when the same analysis type, scope, method, and finding meaning coincide closely. Findings from different analysis runs SHOULD typically remain distinct but MAY be linked or superseded.

## 7. Provenance preservation

Every merge action MUST preserve provenance multiplicity.

### 7.1 Provenance rule

When multiple candidates merge into one canonical object, the canonical object MUST retain references to all source and transformation lineages that contributed to the merge.

### 7.2 Merge event recording

Each merge SHOULD create a provenance or governance event with:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| merge event ID | unique event |
| input object refs | objects considered |
| canonical output ref | retained object |
| merge rationale | why merge occurred |
| method/version | rules or model used |
| operator | human or automated actor |

## 8. Similarity thresholds

The implementation SHOULD use family-specific thresholds.

| Family | Exact threshold | Semantic merge threshold | Cluster threshold |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TextAtom | exact hash match | high | medium |
| EvidenceAtom | exact span+payload | very high | low-medium |
| PolicyAtom | exact or near-exact wording | very high | medium |
| CodeAtom | exact content identity | not recommended for semantic merge | medium-high |
| AnalysisAtom family | exact run+finding identity | high if same method/scope | medium |

Exact numerical values SHOULD be tuned via evaluation harness, not hardcoded in this policy.

## 9. Conflict with trust states

Lower-trust objects MUST NOT silently upgrade higher-trust objects through merge.

Example:

| Case | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| machine-derived analysis matches accepted analysis | attach as supporting provenance or cluster candidate, not overwrite |
| distilled text matches verified text | may attach provenance after review, not downgrade verified status |
| imported external finding matches verified policy atom | never flatten cross-tier objects |

## 10. Merge outputs

The deduplication layer SHOULD emit one of the following decisions for each candidate pair or cluster:

| Decision | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `MERGE_EXACT` | collapse into canonical object |
| `MERGE_SEMANTIC` | merge with provenance preservation |
| `KEEP_SEPARATE_SCOPE` | same meaning, different scope |
| `KEEP_SEPARATE_CONFLICT` | contradiction preserved |
| `LINK_REFINES` | more/less specific |
| `LINK_SUPERSEDES` | historical succession |
| `CLUSTER_SIMILAR` | retrieval grouping only |
| `NO_ACTION` | unrelated or insufficient confidence |

## 11. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 deduplication implementation MUST:

| Requirement | Status |
| --- | --- |
| preserve provenance on merge | mandatory |
| preserve epistemic tier boundaries | mandatory |
| keep contradictions separate | mandatory |
| keep superseded states separate | mandatory |
| treat policy and code merges conservatively | mandatory |
| support similarity clustering without forced merge | strongly recommended |

* * *

# AST Parsing Strategy v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_ast_parsing_strategy_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative strategy

## 1. Purpose

This strategy defines how UAKS-v1 structurally parses code and technical artifacts to produce `CodeAtom`s and dependency relations. The aim is parser-grounded technical atomization with exact spans, stable symbol extraction, and language-aware boundaries.

## 2. Strategy thesis

The code refinery MUST be structurally grounded. It MUST prefer AST or equivalent parse-tree representations for supported languages and structured grammars for supported non-code artifacts.

Regex-only or arbitrary chunking approaches MAY serve as fallback for unsupported inputs, but outputs from those paths SHOULD remain lower-trust until upgraded by parser-grade support.

## 3. Parser tiers

| Tier | Description | Trust |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tier 1 | full AST parser with stable grammar | highest |
| Tier 2 | structured parser or schema-aware parser | high |
| Tier 3 | fallback structural parser | medium-low |
| Tier 4 | text chunk heuristic | low |

## 4. Language support model

The implementation SHOULD maintain a language support registry.

### 4.1 Example support registry

| Family | Preferred parsing mode |
| --- | --- |
| Python | full AST |
| TypeScript / JavaScript | full AST |
| Rust | full AST |
| SQL | grammar-aware structured parse |
| YAML / TOML / JSON | structured parse |
| shell | structured parse where feasible, fallback otherwise |
| Dockerfile / workflow YAML | structured parse |
| Markdown with code fences | mixed-mode parse |

## 5. Parser outputs required for publication

For a supported code object to be published as a normal `CodeAtom`, the parser path SHOULD emit:

| Output | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| symbol identity | name or anonymous stable boundary |
| node type | AST classification |
| source span | exact location |
| container path | file/module/package location |
| child relationships | contained members if applicable |
| dependency indicators | imports, calls, references where possible |

## 6. Boundary rules

### 6.1 Functional boundaries

A function or method SHOULD usually become one `CodeAtom`.

### 6.2 Type boundaries

A class, interface, schema, or migration unit MAY become one `CodeAtom` if operationally meaningful.

### 6.3 Module boundaries

A module MAY become a `CodeAtom` when file-level logic or configuration has meaning beyond contained symbols.

### 6.4 Composite handling

A file MAY produce:

| Result | Example |
| --- | --- |
| one module atom only | small config file |
| module atom plus child function atoms | utility source file |
| class atom plus method atoms | object-oriented source file |
| schema atom plus migration-step atoms | database migration |

## 7. Dependency extraction priorities

The parser strategy SHOULD prioritize these dependency classes:

| Priority | Dependency class |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | imports / requires / use statements |
| 2 | function/method calls |
| 3 | inheritance / interface implementation |
| 4 | query/model/schema usage |
| 5 | config/environment dependency |
| 6 | workflow step dependency |

## 8. Fallback mode rules

When a language lacks Tier 1 or Tier 2 support:

| Rule | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| FP-01 | fallback outputs MUST be marked as lower trust |
| FP-02 | spans MUST still be exact where possible |
| FP-03 | semantic merge of fallback code atoms SHOULD be conservative |
| FP-04 | reprocessing SHOULD be possible when parser support improves |

## 9. Mixed artifact parsing

Some source files contain multiple modalities.

Examples:

| Artifact | Parsing approach |
| --- | --- |
| markdown with code fences | document parse + embedded code parse |
| notebooks | cell-aware parse |
| workflow YAML with shell fragments | structured parse + embedded shell segments |
| template files | container parse + extracted logic blocks |

The parsing strategy SHOULD support multimodal boundary extraction where practical.

## 10. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 AST strategy MUST:

| Requirement | Status |
| --- | --- |
| support parser tiers | mandatory |
| record parser provenance | mandatory |
| preserve exact spans | mandatory |
| distinguish parser-grade from fallback-grade outputs | mandatory |
| support reprocessing on parser improvement | mandatory |

* * *

# Portal Projection Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_portal_projection_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

The portal projection layer provides human-usable and tool-usable views over the atomic substrate without treating those views as primary storage truth. It materializes stable, intelligible workspace views from content-addressed objects, graph relations, and recipes.

## 2. Projection thesis

The substrate stores atoms and immutable content objects. Users and tools often need familiar workspace projections: project trees, file-like structures, documentation bundles, trace reports, code views, and assembled artifacts.

The portal projection layer therefore MUST provide ergonomic structure while preserving the deeper truth that these are projected states, not primary containers.

## 3. Projection objects

| Projection type | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| workspace projection | project/repository-shaped view |
| document projection | rendered text or spec |
| code projection | file/module/function-oriented view |
| trace projection | lineage and relation inspection |
| review projection | validation and dispute workflow view |
| historical projection | time-sliced state view |

## 4. Core projection inputs

| Input | Role |
| --- | --- |
| CAS objects | exact payload states |
| atoms | semantic and technical units |
| entities | conceptual anchors |
| relations | structural map |
| recipes | reusable assembly/projection patterns |
| scope descriptor | project/time/user constraints |

## 5. Projection rules

### 5.1 Identity rule

Projected paths, names, and folders MAY be stable human-facing aliases, but MUST NOT replace content identity or logical atom identity.

### 5.2 Reproducibility rule

A projection SHOULD be reconstructable from its recipe, object references, and scope context.

### 5.3 Non-destructive rule

Edits in a projection layer MUST be mediated back into refinery/governance flows rather than silently mutating canonical substrate truth.

## 6. Projection schema

### 6.1 ProjectionDescriptor

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `projectionId` | unique projection object |
| `projectionType` | workspace, document, code, trace, review, historical |
| `scopeDescriptor` | project/repository/time constraints |
| `rootRefs` | seed atom/entity/CAS refs |
| `recipeRef` | generation recipe if any |
| `createdAt` | creation time |
| `projectionStateHash` | exact projected state identifier |

## 7. Workspace projection behavior

A workspace projection SHOULD provide a familiar hierarchy built from substrate objects.

Possible mapping:

| Projected path concept | Backing substrate |
| --- | --- |
| folder | entity cluster, repo, module, or recipe grouping |
| file | assembled document or code CAS object |
| symbol view | code atom |
| spec section | ordered atom sequence |
| trace panel | graph traversal |

## 8. Historical projection behavior

Historical projections MUST support viewing prior superseded states, prior recipes, and prior code payloads without collapsing them into the current state.

## 9. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 projection layer MUST:

| Requirement | Status |
| --- | --- |
| separate projection identity from canonical identity | mandatory |
| support reproducible projections | mandatory |
| preserve historical state views | mandatory |
| avoid silent truth mutation through projection edits | mandatory |

* * *
