# Template Catalog v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_template_catalog_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative catalog

## 1. Purpose

This catalog defines the canonical output molds used by the Assembly Engine. A template does not create truth. It governs how eligible atoms are arranged, emphasized, and connected for a given communicative purpose.

## 2. Template thesis

The same governed substrate MUST support multiple rendered forms without duplicating underlying truth objects. Templates therefore act as structural molds over atoms, relations, provenance, and recipes.

## 3. Template object model

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | unique template identifier |
| `templateName` | human-readable name |
| `templateClass` | summary, spec, trace, impact, memo, synthesis, review |
| `templateVersion` | version string |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | allowed source families |
| `requiredSections` | mandatory structural sections |
| `optionalSections` | conditional structural sections |
| `resolutionBehavior` | how section depth scales |
| `provenanceMode` | hidden, summary, inline, appendix |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | whether analytical lane may appear |
| `orderingRules` | section and atom sequencing logic |
| `connectiveStyle` | rhetorical connective policy |

## 4. Canonical template classes

| Template class | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Summary | concise understanding |
| TechnicalSpec | formal architecture or implementation explanation |
| TraceReport | lineage, support, or rule-to-code mapping |
| ImpactReport | downstream consequence analysis |
| DecisionMemo | recommendation with rationale |
| ResearchSynthesis | evidence-rich multi-perspective exposition |
| ReviewPacket | adjudication and review workflow support |
| WorkspaceView | portal-facing structured projection |

## 5. Canonical templates

### 5.1 Executive Summary Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_exec_summary_v1` |
| `templateClass` | Summary |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | TextAtom, PolicyAtom, CodeAtom, accepted AnalysisAtom |
| `requiredSections` | topic, core points, implications |
| `resolutionBehavior` | micro/compact favored |
| `provenanceMode` | summary |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | accepted only if materially relevant |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Topic framing | what is being summarized |
| 2 | Core claims | highest-centrality substrate truths |
| 3 | Implications | practical meaning or consequence |

### 5.2 Technical Specification Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_technical_spec_v1` |
| `templateClass` | TechnicalSpec |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | TextAtom, PolicyAtom, CodeAtom, EvidenceAtom, accepted AnalysisAtom |
| `requiredSections` | purpose, scope, model, components, rules |
| `resolutionBehavior` | standard/extended/deep favored |
| `provenanceMode` | appendix or inline |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | only if it clarifies architecture or drift |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Purpose | state what the subsystem does |
| 2 | Scope | define boundaries |
| 3 | Model | explain conceptual structure |
| 4 | Components | enumerate core parts |
| 5 | Rules | normative or operational constraints |
| 6 | Examples | optional concrete objects |
| 7 | Provenance / notes | optional evidence and lineage |

### 5.3 Trace Report Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_trace_report_v1` |
| `templateClass` | TraceReport |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | TextAtom, PolicyAtom, CodeAtom, EvidenceAtom, RelationAtom, accepted AnalysisAtom |
| `requiredSections` | origin, path, targets, support |
| `resolutionBehavior` | standard/deep favored |
| `provenanceMode` | inline or appendix |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | accepted only unless exploratory |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Origin object | starting atom or source |
| 2 | Trace path | graph path narrative |
| 3 | Target objects | downstream or upstream objects |
| 4 | Supporting evidence | support and provenance |
| 5 | Exceptions/conflicts | contradictions or ambiguities |

### 5.4 Impact Report Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_impact_report_v1` |
| `templateClass` | ImpactReport |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | CodeAtom, PolicyAtom, TextAtom, accepted DriftAtom, accepted AnalysisAtom |
| `requiredSections` | change trigger, affected objects, risk, actions |
| `resolutionBehavior` | compact/standard/extended |
| `provenanceMode` | summary or appendix |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | accepted analytical triggers allowed |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Trigger | what changed |
| 2 | Affected scope | impacted objects and areas |
| 3 | Risk profile | severity and uncertainty |
| 4 | Recommended actions | review, update, test, reclassify |
| 5 | Trace appendix | optional detailed path |

### 5.5 Decision Memo Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_decision_memo_v1` |
| `templateClass` | DecisionMemo |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | TextAtom, PolicyAtom, CodeAtom, EvidenceAtom, accepted AnalysisAtom |
| `requiredSections` | question, options, evaluation, recommendation |
| `resolutionBehavior` | standard/extended |
| `provenanceMode` | summary or appendix |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | accepted analytical support allowed |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Decision question | define the issue |
| 2 | Options | alternatives under consideration |
| 3 | Evaluation | compare by evidence and constraints |
| 4 | Recommendation | selected path |
| 5 | Rationale | why this path follows |
| 6 | Risks | known costs or uncertainties |

### 5.6 Research Synthesis Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_research_synthesis_v1` |
| `templateClass` | ResearchSynthesis |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | all primary atoms plus reviewed/accepted analytical atoms |
| `requiredSections` | framing, evidence lattice, interpretations, tensions, synthesis |
| `resolutionBehavior` | extended/deep favored |
| `provenanceMode` | inline and appendix |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | reviewed and accepted, experimental only if explicitly requested |

Section order:

| Order | Section | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Research framing | topic and scope |
| 2 | Core evidence | source-grounded objects |
| 3 | Interpretive layer | governed interpretations |
| 4 | Tensions and contradictions | unresolved or competing states |
| 5 | Synthesis | integrated conclusion |
| 6 | Further questions | optional next research lanes |

### 5.7 Review Packet Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_review_packet_v1` |
| `templateClass` | ReviewPacket |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | any disputable or promotable family |
| `requiredSections` | object summary, provenance, issues, proposed disposition |
| `resolutionBehavior` | standard |
| `provenanceMode` | inline |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | include as relevant to adjudication |

### 5.8 Workspace Projection Template

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `templateId` | `template_workspace_projection_v1` |
| `templateClass` | WorkspaceView |
| `eligibleAtomFamilies` | TextAtom, CodeAtom, RecipeAtom, Entity |
| `requiredSections` | root, groupings, nodes, links |
| `resolutionBehavior` | compact/standard |
| `provenanceMode` | hidden by default |
| `analysisInclusionPolicy` | excluded by default |

## 6. Resolution behavior rules

| Resolution | Template behavior |
| --- | --- |
| MICRO | core sections only, highest-density atoms |
| COMPACT | essential sections, minimal support |
| STANDARD | balanced section set |
| EXTENDED | full section set with explanatory connective tissue |
| DEEP | full sections plus evidence, contradictions, lineage |

## 7. Template selection policy

| Query or task type | Preferred template |
| --- | --- |
| concise overview | Executive Summary |
| architecture/system design | Technical Specification |
| what leads to what | Trace Report |
| what changes if X changes | Impact Report |
| choose among options | Decision Memo |
| deep literature / evidence integration | Research Synthesis |
| adjudicate trust/state | Review Packet |
| project-style browsing | Workspace Projection |

## 8. Final normative judgment

A valid template implementation MUST separate template structure from underlying truth objects, MUST be resolution-aware, MUST honor trust policy, and SHOULD preserve provenance rendering options.

* * *

# Provenance Rendering Rules v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_provenance_rendering_rules_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative rules

## 1. Purpose

These rules define how provenance is surfaced in rendered outputs, trace reports, review packets, portal projections, and inspection interfaces.

## 2. Provenance rendering thesis

Provenance must remain available without overwhelming every output. The system therefore supports graded provenance visibility based on output mode, template class, and trust context.

## 3. Provenance modes

| Mode | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Hidden | provenance stored but not shown |
| Summary | high-level source summary shown |
| Inline | provenance attached near statements |
| Appendix | detailed lineage attached after main content |
| Forensic | full lineage, spans, operations, and states shown |

## 4. Output-mode defaults

| Output mode | Default provenance mode |
| --- | --- |
| Standard operational | Summary |
| Research | Inline or Appendix |
| Exploratory | Appendix |
| High-trust / compliance | Inline and Appendix |
| Review / adjudication | Forensic |

## 5. Minimum provenance disclosure rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| PRR-01 | every rendered output SHOULD be able to resolve back to atom IDs used |
| PRR-02 | high-trust outputs MUST expose source and validation lineage |
| PRR-03 | analytical findings MUST be distinguishable from source-grounded claims |
| PRR-04 | superseded or disputed objects MUST be visibly marked when shown |
| PRR-05 | provenance summaries MUST not imply stronger certainty than underlying states justify |

## 6. Statement-level rendering rules

| Case | Rendering requirement |
| --- | --- |
| source-grounded primary claim | show source summary or inline reference when needed |
| accepted analysis finding | mark as analytical/derived |
| reviewed interpretation | mark inferential status where relevant |
| disputed object | visibly flag dispute |
| historical object | visibly flag non-current status |

## 7. Provenance packet structure

A provenance packet SHOULD include:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `atomId` | governing atom reference |
| `validationState` | trust state |
| `sourceRefs` | raw source anchors |
| `sourceSpans` | exact locations where available |
| `provenanceRefs` | transformation lineage |
| `supersessionStatus` | current/historical |
| `analysisRunRef` | if analytical derivative |
| `warnings` | ambiguity, stale state, low trust |

## 8. Rendering examples

### 8.1 Summary mode

Main statement followed by concise source note.

### 8.2 Inline mode

Main statement followed by compact provenance markers or structured parenthetical metadata.

### 8.3 Appendix mode

Main body remains readable; detailed atom and source table appears after body.

### 8.4 Forensic mode

Each claim is paired with atom identity, source span, transformation history, and trust state.

## 9. Final normative judgment

A valid provenance rendering implementation MUST preserve the distinction between source evidence and derivative analysis, MUST support stronger disclosure in higher-trust contexts, and SHOULD avoid clutter in low-friction summaries.

* * *

# Traceability Report Format v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_traceability_report_format_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative format

## 1. Purpose

This format standardizes reports that explain how one object or claim is connected to others across the substrate. It is used for rule-to-code trace, source-to-claim trace, claim-to-evidence trace, and analysis-to-review trace.

## 2. Report thesis

A trace report is not merely a list of links. It is a structured explanation of path, support, state, and consequence.

## 3. Core report schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `reportId` | unique report identifier |
| `reportType` | rule_to_code, source_to_claim, claim_to_evidence, analysis_to_review, historical_trace |
| `originRef` | starting object |
| `targetRefs` | reached objects |
| `pathRefs` | relation edges and intermediate nodes |
| `stateSummary` | current trust/supersession status |
| `provenanceMode` | hidden/summary/inline/appendix/forensic |
| `generatedAt` | timestamp |
| `recipeRef` | if persisted |

## 4. Canonical sections

| Order | Section | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Trace objective | Yes |
| 2 | Origin object summary | Yes |
| 3 | Path narrative | Yes |
| 4 | Target object summaries | Yes |
| 5 | Support and provenance | Yes |
| 6 | Exceptions, ambiguity, or conflict | Conditional |
| 7 | Historical notes | Conditional |

## 5. Trace types

### 5.1 Rule-to-code trace

| Element | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Origin | PolicyAtom or TextAtom |
| Path | AFFECTS / IMPLEMENTS / DOCUMENTS / TESTS edges |
| Targets | CodeAtom(s), test atoms, supporting docs |
| Notes | missing implementations, disputed links, stale code states |

### 5.2 Source-to-claim trace

| Element | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Origin | SourceObject or EvidenceAtom |
| Path | DERIVED_FROM / SUPPORTED_BY / REFINES |
| Targets | TextAtom, PolicyAtom, InterpretationAtom |
| Notes | scope and transformation history |

### 5.3 Claim-to-evidence trace

| Element | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Origin | TextAtom or PolicyAtom |
| Path | SUPPORTED_BY |
| Targets | EvidenceAtom(s), source spans |
| Notes | evidence sufficiency and gaps |

### 5.4 Analysis-to-review trace

| Element | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Origin | DriftAtom, InterpretationAtom, AnalysisAtom |
| Path | REQUIRES_REVIEW_OF / MAPS_TO / ANALYZES |
| Targets | objects flagged for review |
| Notes | trust status, run provenance, scope |

## 6. Trace quality rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| TRF-01 | a trace report MUST distinguish direct edges from inferred narrative explanation |
| TRF-02 | a trace report MUST surface missing links if the chain is incomplete |
| TRF-03 | a trace report SHOULD mark whether targets are current, superseded, disputed, or accepted |
| TRF-04 | a trace report MUST not collapse contradictory branches without flagging them |

## 7. Minimal trace result example

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Origin | `atom_policy_005` |
| Path | `AFFECTS -> atom_code_021`, `TESTS -> atom_code_test_003` |
| Targets | implementation function, linked test |
| Notes | one dependent code path lacks reviewed test coverage |

## 8. Final normative judgment

A valid traceability report MUST be path-aware, state-aware, and provenance-aware. It SHOULD be readable both as a structured table and as narrative explanation.

* * *

# Impact Analysis Report Format v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_impact_analysis_report_format_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative format

## 1. Purpose

This format standardizes reports that explain likely downstream consequences of a change in policy, code, ontology, analysis findings, or other substrate objects.

## 2. Report thesis

Impact analysis is not simply dependency listing. It is structured consequence analysis combining direct dependency paths, risk, trust state, and recommended action.

## 3. Core report schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `reportId` | unique report identifier |
| `triggerRef` | changed or proposed-to-change object |
| `affectedRefs` | impacted objects |
| `impactPaths` | graph paths supporting impact claims |
| `riskSummary` | severity and uncertainty |
| `recommendedActions` | next steps |
| `generatedAt` | timestamp |
| `recipeRef` | persisted recipe if any |

## 4. Canonical sections

| Order | Section | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Trigger summary | Yes |
| 2 | Affected scope | Yes |
| 3 | Path-based impact explanation | Yes |
| 4 | Risk classification | Yes |
| 5 | Recommended actions | Yes |
| 6 | Supporting provenance | Yes |
| 7 | Historical / supersession context | Conditional |

## 5. Impact trigger classes

| Trigger class | Typical impact targets |
| --- | --- |
| PolicyAtom change | CodeAtom, docs, tests, workflows |
| CodeAtom change | dependents, tests, interfaces, docs |
| DriftAtom accepted finding | taxonomy, policy, reclassification targets |
| TaxonomyProposalAtom adoption | entity classes, retrieval behavior, templates |
| Recipe/template change | portal views, rendered outputs, downstream clients |

## 6. Risk model

| Risk level | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Low | localized, low uncertainty |
| Medium | multiple dependents or unclear edge effects |
| High | broad dependency spread, policy/compliance implications, or high-trust outputs affected |

Risk SHOULD consider:

| Factor | Influence |
| --- | --- |
| dependency breadth | upward |
| trust criticality | upward |
| missing test/support coverage | upward |
| accepted historical stability | downward |
| clear rollback path | downward |

## 7. Recommended action classes

| Action | Use |
| --- | --- |
| review | human adjudication needed |
| update_code | implementing logic must change |
| update_policy | normative rule must change |
| reclassify | ontology or trust state must change |
| rerun_analysis | analytical confirmation needed |
| add_test | insufficient validation coverage |
| no_action | impact determined minimal |

## 8. Quality rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| IAF-01 | an impact report MUST distinguish direct from indirect impact |
| IAF-02 | an impact report SHOULD include uncertainty notes where graph paths are incomplete |
| IAF-03 | an impact report MUST surface high-trust affected objects clearly |
| IAF-04 | an impact report SHOULD name missing evidence or missing test coverage where relevant |

## 9. Minimal example

| Section | Example content |
| --- | --- |
| Trigger | accepted semantic drift finding about “validation” |
| Affected scope | taxonomy node, policy atom, two implementing code atoms |
| Risk | medium-high because policy-code semantics may have diverged |
| Recommended actions | taxonomy review, policy review, code trace audit, add targeted tests |

## 10. Final normative judgment

A valid impact report MUST be consequence-aware, path-aware, and action-oriented. It SHOULD explain not only what is affected, but why and what should happen next.

* * *

# Resolver Contract Specification v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_resolver_contract_spec_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative specification

## 1. Purpose

This specification defines the behavior of GraphQL resolvers and adjacent query handlers over the UAKS-v1 substrate. It exists to ensure that API behavior remains consistent with ontology, trust policy, provenance rules, and storage boundaries.

## 2. Resolver thesis

Resolvers are contract surfaces, not truth sources. They orchestrate retrieval, filtering, joining, projection, and rendering over authoritative substrate layers.

## 3. Resolver responsibilities

| Responsibility | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| object resolution | map IDs to canonical objects |
| policy enforcement | apply validation/access/trust gates |
| hybrid retrieval orchestration | invoke lexical, semantic, graph channels |
| projection | shape results into API contract |
| provenance attachment | expose lineage according to mode |
| error signaling | distinguish not found, unauthorized, filtered, ambiguous |

## 4. Resolver classes

| Resolver class | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| direct object resolver | fetch one object by ID |
| collection resolver | search/filter paginated object sets |
| trace resolver | produce path-aware trace results |
| impact resolver | produce consequence-aware results |
| assembly resolver | produce rendered text and recipe-aware outputs |
| import mutation resolver | ingest external analysis bundles |
| relation mutation resolver | create governed relations |

## 5. Core contract rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| RCS-01 | resolvers MUST not bypass validation/trust policy |
| RCS-02 | resolvers MUST not invent substrate objects absent from authoritative storage |
| RCS-03 | resolvers SHOULD return score breakdowns or match reasons where practical for ranked retrieval |
| RCS-04 | resolvers MUST distinguish current from superseded/historical objects when relevant |
| RCS-05 | resolvers MUST preserve ontology distinctions across evidence, primary atoms, and analysis atoms |

## 6. Query resolver behavior

### 6.1 `atom(id)`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| direct ID resolution | fetch canonical object by ID |
| trust handling | enforce access, not assembly eligibility |
| historical behavior | return object even if superseded, clearly marked |

### 6.2 `atoms(filter, limit, offset)`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| candidate generation | hybrid retrieval if `textQuery` present |
| filter application | type, state, scope, source, entity filters |
| pagination | deterministic and stable |
| explanation | SHOULD include match reasons in debug/inspection modes |

### 6.3 `traceRuleToCode(ruleAtomId)`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| origin validation | confirm origin is rule-like object |
| path traversal | prioritize AFFECTS, IMPLEMENTS, DOCUMENTS, TESTS |
| result shape | path-aware and target-aware trace result |
| missing links | return explicit warnings, not silent omission |

### 6.4 `impactAnalysis(codeAtomId)`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| dependency traversal | downstream and linked rule/doc/test paths |
| risk shaping | classify risk and uncertainty |
| result shape | structured consequence result |

### 6.5 `assemble(input)`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| retrieval | invoke scoring specification if query-driven |
| policy filtering | enforce output-mode trust rules |
| template application | honor requested template |
| provenance mode | attach according to template and mode |
| output | return rendered text plus atom/provenance references |

## 7. Mutation resolver behavior

### 7.1 `createRelationEdge`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| ontology validation | relation type must be canonical or approved |
| endpoint validation | source and target must exist |
| provenance capture | mutation should record actor/time/provenance |
| policy enforcement | restricted by access and trust rules |

### 7.2 `importAnalysisBundle`

| Requirement | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| schema validation | envelope must satisfy import contract |
| normalization membrane | MUST be applied |
| output | return AnalysisRunAtom and imported object references |
| trust state | imported objects enter with imported/machine-derived states, not accepted automatically |

## 8. Error contract

Resolvers SHOULD classify errors into stable categories.

| Error class | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `NOT_FOUND` | no such object exists |
| `UNAUTHORIZED` | caller lacks access |
| `FILTERED_BY_POLICY` | object exists but is not eligible under requested mode |
| `INVALID_INPUT` | malformed request |
| `AMBIGUOUS_QUERY` | request resolves to multiple incompatible anchors |
| `INCOMPLETE_TRACE` | result exists but graph path is incomplete |
| `IMPORT_REJECTED` | analysis bundle failed schema or trust checks |

## 9. Performance and batching rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| RCS-P-01 | resolvers SHOULD batch related object fetches |
| RCS-P-02 | resolvers SHOULD avoid N+1 graph traversals where possible |
| RCS-P-03 | ranked collection queries SHOULD cap candidate pools before expensive deep expansion |
| RCS-P-04 | provenance expansion SHOULD be lazy unless requested or required by mode |

## 10. Final normative judgment

A valid resolver implementation MUST behave as a disciplined orchestration layer over the authoritative substrate. It MUST respect ontology, trust, provenance, and history semantics, and SHOULD expose enough explanation to make ranked retrieval and trace behavior auditable.

# Query Pattern Library v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_query_pattern_library_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative library

## 1. Purpose

This library defines canonical query patterns for retrieval, trace, impact, review, assembly, and portal projection. It exists to standardize how clients, resolvers, operators, and evaluation harnesses express common intents against the substrate.

## 2. Query pattern thesis

A stable knowledge substrate needs stable query archetypes. Free-form querying remains useful, but core workflows SHOULD resolve through repeatable query patterns with known semantics, expected result types, and predictable trust behavior.

## 3. Query pattern object model

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | unique pattern identifier |
| `patternName` | human-readable label |
| `intentClass` | fact, policy, code, trace, impact, research, review, projection |
| `inputShape` | expected input fields |
| `defaultFilters` | type/state/scope defaults |
| `retrievalProfile` | weighting profile to apply |
| `outputTemplate` | preferred template if assembled |
| `trustMode` | standard, research, exploratory, high-trust |
| `resultShape` | expected response structure |

## 4. Canonical query families

| Family | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Direct Lookup | fetch exact object by ID or exact identifier |
| Concept Retrieval | find governed objects about a topic |
| Policy Retrieval | find rules, constraints, and implementing logic |
| Code Retrieval | find symbols, modules, dependencies, tests |
| Trace Retrieval | explain how one object connects to another |
| Impact Retrieval | explain likely consequences of a change |
| Research Retrieval | broad evidence-rich synthesis candidate pool |
| Review Retrieval | adjudicate disputed or promotable objects |
| Projection Retrieval | construct workspace or document views |

## 5. Canonical patterns

### 5.1 Exact Object Lookup

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_exact_object_lookup_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Direct Lookup |
| `inputShape` | `id` |
| `defaultFilters` | none beyond access control |
| `retrievalProfile` | direct resolution |
| `resultShape` | single object |

Expected behavior: resolve by canonical object ID, return current or historical object with clear state markers.

### 5.2 Exact Symbol Lookup

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_exact_symbol_lookup_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Code Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `symbolName`, optional `repositoryId`, `modulePath` |
| `defaultFilters` | `CodeAtom`, active by default |
| `retrievalProfile` | code retrieval |
| `resultShape` | ranked code atoms plus dependency context |

Expected behavior: favor lexical symbol match, then graph expansion for dependencies and tests.

### 5.3 Topic Concept Retrieval

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_topic_concept_retrieval_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Concept Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `queryText`, optional `scopeDescriptor` |
| `defaultFilters` | primary atoms, verified/accepted by default |
| `retrievalProfile` | balanced |
| `resultShape` | ranked atom set |

Expected behavior: balanced lexical-semantic retrieval with light graph expansion.

### 5.4 Policy-to-Code Trace

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_policy_to_code_trace_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Trace Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `policyAtomId` or `queryText` resolving to rule-like object |
| `defaultFilters` | `PolicyAtom`, `TextAtom`, `CodeAtom`, supporting relations |
| `retrievalProfile` | trace and impact |
| `outputTemplate` | Trace Report |
| `resultShape` | origin, paths, target code, tests, gaps |

Expected behavior: traverse `AFFECTS`, `IMPLEMENTS`, `DOCUMENTS`, `TESTS`.

### 5.5 Claim-to-Evidence Trace

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_claim_to_evidence_trace_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Trace Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `atomId` or claim query |
| `defaultFilters` | `TextAtom`, `EvidenceAtom`, support relations |
| `retrievalProfile` | trace |
| `outputTemplate` | Trace Report |
| `resultShape` | claim, supporting evidence, provenance notes |

### 5.6 Change Impact Query

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_change_impact_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Impact Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `triggerRef`, optional `scopeDescriptor`, optional `depth` |
| `defaultFilters` | impacted code, policy, docs, tests |
| `retrievalProfile` | trace and impact |
| `outputTemplate` | Impact Report |
| `resultShape` | affected scope, risk, actions |

### 5.7 Drift Review Query

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_drift_review_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Review Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `driftAtomId` or analytical query with includeAnalysis |
| `defaultFilters` | accepted/reviewed analysis plus linked primary atoms |
| `retrievalProfile` | research deep or trace |
| `outputTemplate` | Review Packet |
| `resultShape` | drift finding, affected objects, recommended review set |

### 5.8 Deep Research Synthesis Query

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_deep_research_synthesis_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Research Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `queryText`, `scopeDescriptor`, optional time window |
| `defaultFilters` | verified/reviewed primary atoms, accepted/reviewed analysis |
| `retrievalProfile` | research deep |
| `outputTemplate` | Research Synthesis |
| `resultShape` | evidence lattice, interpretations, tensions, synthesis |

### 5.9 Workspace Projection Query

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `patternId` | `qp_workspace_projection_v1` |
| `intentClass` | Projection Retrieval |
| `inputShape` | `projectId` or `rootRefs`, optional historical state |
| `defaultFilters` | active code/text/recipe/entity objects |
| `retrievalProfile` | mixed projection profile |
| `outputTemplate` | Workspace Projection |
| `resultShape` | projection descriptor with grouped nodes |

## 6. Pattern-specific defaults

| Pattern | Default trust mode | Include analysis | Historical by default |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| exact object lookup | inspection | no | yes if ID resolves historical |
| exact symbol lookup | standard | no | no |
| topic concept retrieval | standard | no | no |
| policy-to-code trace | standard | no | no |
| claim-to-evidence trace | standard | no | no |
| change impact | standard | accepted only if relevant | no |
| drift review | research | yes | sometimes |
| deep research synthesis | research | yes | optional |
| workspace projection | standard | no | optional |

## 7. Evaluation use

Each pattern SHOULD have corresponding gold queries and expected object sets in the evaluation harness.

## 8. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 query implementation SHOULD route common intents through this library, MUST respect pattern-level trust and scope defaults, and SHOULD expose pattern IDs in logs and evaluation traces.

* * *

# Auth and Access Model v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_auth_access_model_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative model

## 1. Purpose

This model defines authentication assumptions, authorization layers, access scopes, and result-filtering behavior for UAKS-v1. It governs who may view, query, assemble, mutate, review, or import across the substrate.

## 2. Access thesis

Authorization in UAKS-v1 MUST apply at more than one layer. It is not sufficient to secure only the API. Access decisions MUST be compatible with source sensitivity, atom trust state, projection context, and mutation risk.

## 3. Access layers

| Layer | Function |
| --- | --- |
| Identity layer | authenticate actor |
| Role layer | assign functional role |
| Scope layer | restrict project/repository/domain reach |
| Object layer | restrict source/atom/entity visibility |
| Action layer | restrict operations such as import, review, relation creation |
| Output layer | restrict what may appear in assemblies or projections |

## 4. Actor classes

| Actor class | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Reader | may inspect allowed objects |
| Researcher | may access broader read-only analytical contexts |
| Reviewer | may adjudicate trust states and review packets |
| Editor | may submit structured changes and new relations |
| Importer | may submit external analysis bundles |
| Operator | may manage pipelines and release operations |
| Administrator | may manage policy and access controls |
| ServiceAccount | non-human system actor with constrained privileges |

## 5. Core permissions

| Permission | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `read_object` | inspect object by ID |
| `query_collection` | run filtered retrieval |
| `assemble_output` | generate rendered outputs |
| `view_provenance` | inspect lineage details |
| `view_forensic` | inspect full forensic provenance |
| `create_relation` | create governed relations |
| `import_analysis` | submit external analysis envelope |
| `change_validation_state` | promote or dispute objects |
| `manage_projection` | create and persist projections |
| `manage_policy` | update governance rules |
| `operate_pipeline` | run refinery or import jobs |

## 6. Scope descriptors for access

Authorization SHOULD operate with scopes such as:

| Scope type | Example |
| --- | --- |
| project scope | `project_alpha` |
| repository scope | `repo_refinery` |
| source scope | specific source IDs |
| domain scope | policy, code, research, operations |
| sensitivity scope | public, internal, restricted, privileged |
| environment scope | development, staging, production |

## 7. Object sensitivity classes

Every stored object SHOULD carry a sensitivity class.

| Sensitivity class | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Public | broadly viewable |
| Internal | authenticated internal readers |
| Restricted | limited by role and scope |
| Privileged | limited to reviewers/operators/admins |
| Sealed | accessible only via special policy or emergency route |

## 8. Access decision model

An access decision SHOULD be computed as:

| Factor | Question |
| --- | --- |
| authenticated | who is the actor |
| role fit | does the role allow the requested action |
| scope fit | does the actor have required project/domain scope |
| sensitivity fit | is the object too sensitive for this actor |
| trust fit | may the actor see low-trust or disputed objects |
| mode fit | is the output mode allowed for this actor |

## 9. Read rules

### 9.1 Direct object read

A direct object read MAY succeed even for superseded or disputed objects if the actor has inspection rights. Read access and normal assembly eligibility are distinct concepts.

### 9.2 Collection query read

Collection queries MUST filter inaccessible objects before ranking and return stable semantics for filtered results.

### 9.3 Provenance read

Forensic provenance SHOULD be more restricted than summary provenance.

| Provenance level | Minimum role suggestion |
| --- | --- |
| summary | Reader |
| inline | Reader / Researcher |
| appendix | Researcher / Reviewer |
| forensic | Reviewer / Operator / Administrator |

## 10. Mutation rules

| Mutation | Minimum role |
| --- | --- |
| create relation | Editor or above |
| import analysis | Importer or above |
| promote validation state | Reviewer or above |
| dispute object | Reviewer or above |
| change governance policy | Administrator |
| rerun or operate pipeline | Operator or Administrator |

## 11. Analytical lane access

Because the analytical lane is derivative and may contain unstable interpretations, its visibility SHOULD be role- and mode-aware.

| Analysis state | Typical visibility |
| --- | --- |
| IMPORTED | Importer, Reviewer, Operator |
| MACHINE_DERIVED | Researcher, Reviewer, Operator |
| REVIEWED | Researcher, Reviewer, Operator |
| ACCEPTED | same as primary substrate visibility unless sensitivity says otherwise |
| EXPERIMENTAL | Researcher, Reviewer, Operator |
| DISPUTED | Reviewer, Operator, Administrator by default |

## 12. Projection access

A projection MUST NOT reveal objects the actor could not independently access. Projection convenience does not override object-level authorization.

## 13. Error semantics

| Access error | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| unauthorized | not authenticated or invalid identity |
| forbidden | authenticated but lacks permission |
| filtered | object exists but removed by policy from this result |
| redacted | object partially accessible but sensitive fields hidden |

## 14. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 access implementation MUST enforce authorization across query, assembly, provenance, mutation, and projection layers, MUST not let projections bypass object restrictions, and SHOULD distinguish clearly between visibility and assembly eligibility.

* * *

# Portal Projection Data Model v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_portal_projection_data_model_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft normative data model

## 1. Purpose

This data model defines the object structures used to create, store, inspect, and reconstruct workspace, document, trace, review, and historical projections over the substrate.

## 2. Projection thesis

A projection is a structured view, not a source of truth. The model therefore separates projection identity, projection state, projection nodes, and canonical substrate references.

## 3. Core projection objects

| Object | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `ProjectionDescriptor` | root metadata for one projection |
| `ProjectionNode` | one visible node within a projection |
| `ProjectionGroup` | hierarchical grouping or container |
| `ProjectionEdge` | visible relation between projection nodes |
| `ProjectionRecipeBinding` | link between projection and recipe |
| `ProjectionStateSnapshot` | reconstructable state hash and object refs |

## 4. ProjectionDescriptor

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `projectionId` | unique projection identifier |
| `projectionType` | workspace, document, code, trace, review, historical |
| `scopeDescriptor` | scope constraints used to build it |
| `rootRefs` | seed object references |
| `recipeRef` | optional driving recipe |
| `projectionStateHash` | exact state identifier |
| `createdAt` | creation time |
| `createdBy` | actor or service |
| `visibilityClass` | access label |
| `status` | active, archived, superseded |

## 5. ProjectionNode

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `projectionNodeId` | unique node within projection |
| `projectionId` | parent projection |
| `nodeType` | folder, file, symbol, section, report_block, entity_ref |
| `label` | displayed name |
| `canonicalRef` | underlying atom/entity/CAS/source/recipe ref |
| `parentGroupId` | containing group if any |
| `ordinal` | ordering index |
| `stateHash` | rendered or projected state hash |
| `renderMetadata` | display metadata |
| `accessFiltered` | whether portions are hidden/redacted |

## 6. ProjectionGroup

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `projectionGroupId` | unique group identifier |
| `projectionId` | parent projection |
| `groupType` | workspace_folder, document_section, trace_segment, review_bucket |
| `label` | displayed grouping label |
| `parentGroupId` | nesting parent |
| `ordinal` | ordering index |

## 7. ProjectionEdge

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `projectionEdgeId` | unique projection edge |
| `projectionId` | parent projection |
| `sourceNodeId` | node origin |
| `targetNodeId` | node target |
| `edgeType` | visible relation or navigation link |
| `canonicalRelationRef` | optional backing relation edge |
| `renderMetadata` | display metadata |

## 8. ProjectionRecipeBinding

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `bindingId` | unique binding |
| `projectionId` | target projection |
| `recipeRef` | recipe used |
| `templateRef` | template used |
| `resolutionLevel` | projection depth |
| `generatedAt` | timestamp |

## 9. ProjectionStateSnapshot

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `snapshotId` | unique snapshot |
| `projectionId` | owning projection |
| `projectionStateHash` | exact projection state hash |
| `includedRefs` | canonical refs included |
| `generatedAt` | timestamp |
| `historicalMode` | whether snapshot reflects non-current state |
| `notes` | optional generation notes |

## 10. Projection node types

| Node type | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| folder | grouping container in workspace view |
| file | document or code-like projection |
| symbol | code-level symbol representation |
| section | document section |
| report_block | report subsection |
| entity_ref | conceptual anchor |
| trace_node | node in a trace path |
| review_item | item under adjudication |

## 11. Projection generation rules

| Rule ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| PDM-01 | every projection node MUST retain a canonical reference or explicitly declare itself synthetic |
| PDM-02 | synthetic connective nodes MUST be marked as synthetic |
| PDM-03 | historical projections MUST declare historical mode |
| PDM-04 | projection hashes SHOULD change when included refs or render-relevant ordering changes |
| PDM-05 | redacted projections MUST mark filtered nodes or fields |

## 12. Minimal example

JSON

```
{  "projectionId": "proj_workspace_001",  "projectionType": "workspace",  "scopeDescriptor": {    "projectIds": ["project_uaks"]  },  "rootRefs": ["entity_project_uaks"],  "recipeRef": "recipe_workspace_default_v1",  "projectionStateHash": "sha256:proj001",  "createdAt": "2026-03-11T17:00:00Z",  "createdBy": "svc_projection",  "visibilityClass": "internal",  "status": "active"}
```

## 13. Final normative judgment

A valid portal projection model MUST preserve reconstructability, canonical references, access compatibility, and historical visibility, while remaining explicitly secondary to the underlying substrate.

* * *

# Pilot Intake SOP v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_pilot_intake_sop_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft standard operating procedure

## 1. Purpose

This SOP defines the intake process for pilot-phase sources, technical artifacts, and external analytical bundles. It ensures that incoming material enters the system in a controlled, auditable, and reviewable way.

## 2. Pilot intake objective

The pilot phase is not just ingestion for volume. It is ingestion for learning. Intake therefore MUST preserve traceability, isolate ambiguous cases, and produce useful signals for schema, refinery, and governance tuning.

## 3. Intake categories

| Intake category | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| textual source intake | memos, notes, transcripts, specs |
| technical source intake | code repositories, scripts, config, schemas |
| external analysis intake | analytical bundles from linguistic or parallel systems |
| seed corpus intake | curated evaluation or canonical examples |

## 4. Intake roles

| Role | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Intake operator | registers and stages incoming objects |
| Reviewer | adjudicates ambiguous or sensitive intake |
| Pipeline operator | runs refinery or import jobs |
| Governance reviewer | resolves policy/trust issues |

## 5. Intake phases

| Phase | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PI-01 | Submission | receive incoming material |
| PI-02 | Registration | assign source identity and metadata |
| PI-03 | Classification | determine source family, sensitivity, scope |
| PI-04 | Staging | place in pilot intake queue |
| PI-05 | Preflight checks | validate completeness and parseability |
| PI-06 | Refinery/import execution | run text/code/analysis intake path |
| PI-07 | Post-run review | inspect output quality and anomalies |
| PI-08 | Publication or hold | release to pilot substrate or quarantine |

## 6. Submission requirements

Every intake submission SHOULD include, where applicable:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| source description | what is being submitted |
| source origin | where it came from |
| expected category | text, code, analysis, seed corpus |
| project/repository scope | contextual boundary |
| sensitivity class | public/internal/restricted/etc. |
| author or owner | if known |
| notes on known issues | parse problems, incomplete spans, ambiguity |

## 7. Registration steps

At registration, the intake operator MUST:

| Step | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| assign source ID | mandatory |
| compute checksum | mandatory |
| assign MIME/type | mandatory |
| assign raw archive reference | mandatory |
| record intake timestamp | mandatory |
| assign initial scope and sensitivity | mandatory |

## 8. Preflight checks

### 8.1 Textual intake checks

| Check | Pass condition |
| --- | --- |
| extractable text present | yes |
| structure preserved enough for segmentation | yes |
| source metadata recorded | yes |
| sensitivity assigned | yes |

### 8.2 Technical intake checks

| Check | Pass condition |
| --- | --- |
| repository or file set accessible | yes |
| language family detectable | yes |
| parser tier known or fallback declared | yes |
| source exactness preserved | yes |

### 8.3 External analysis intake checks

| Check | Pass condition |
| --- | --- |
| envelope schema valid | yes |
| analysis run metadata present | yes |
| source scope present | yes |
| findings array present | yes |
| normalization path available | yes |

## 9. Intake decision outcomes

| Outcome | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| publish_to_pilot | proceed into substrate |
| publish_with_review_flag | proceed but require review |
| quarantine | hold pending missing information or trust concern |
| reject | malformed or non-conforming submission |
| defer | out of pilot scope |

## 10. Required logging

Each intake job MUST log:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| intake job ID | unique intake event |
| submitted refs | sources included |
| operator | actor |
| path chosen | text/code/analysis |
| preflight result | pass/fail |
| output counts | objects produced |
| anomalies | parse failures, trust mismatches, dedup issues |
| final outcome | publish/quarantine/etc. |

## 11. Pilot metrics

Pilot intake SHOULD measure:

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| source acceptance rate | submissions that pass |
| parse success rate | successful refinery start |
| atom yield | objects produced per source |
| review flag rate | ambiguity/trust burden |
| quarantine rate | problematic intake frequency |
| time to publish | operational throughput |

## 12. Final normative judgment

A valid pilot intake process MUST register every source before processing, MUST log preflight and outcome decisions, MUST support quarantine for ambiguous cases, and SHOULD optimize for auditability over speed.

* * *

# Review Workflow SOP v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_review_workflow_sop_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Draft standard operating procedure

## 1. Purpose

This SOP defines how atoms, relations, analysis findings, and projections move through review, adjudication, promotion, dispute, and acceptance workflows.

## 2. Review thesis

The substrate gains trust not from existence but from adjudication. Review is therefore a core system function, not a cosmetic afterthought.

## 3. Review targets

| Review target | Typical trigger |
| --- | --- |
| `PolicyAtom` | normative consequence |
| `CodeAtom` | high-risk implementation or unclear dependency |
| `InterpretationAtom` | inferential claim |
| `DriftAtom` | taxonomy or policy impact |
| relation edge | contested or high-consequence linkage |
| projection/report | human-facing artifact used for decision-making |

## 4. Review roles

| Role | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Reviewer | inspects object and proposes disposition |
| Senior reviewer | resolves ambiguity or escalations |
| Operator | supports tooling and workflow state |
| Administrator | handles policy exceptions |

## 5. Review states

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| queued | awaiting review |
| in_review | actively being inspected |
| approved | passes review |
| needs_revision | insufficient as-is |
| disputed | materially challenged |
| accepted | approved for normal use in analysis lane |
| verified | approved for normal use in primary substrate |
| deferred | postponed pending further evidence |

## 6. Review packet contents

A review packet SHOULD include:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| target ref | object under review |
| object summary | concise explanation |
| provenance packet | source and transformation lineage |
| trust state | current state |
| issues list | open concerns |
| supporting links | evidence, related objects, trace paths |
| proposed disposition | approve, revise, dispute, defer |

## 7. Review workflow phases

| Phase | Name | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RW-01 | Queueing | create review item |
| RW-02 | Triage | prioritize by risk and consequence |
| RW-03 | Inspection | inspect content, provenance, relations, scope |
| RW-04 | Cross-checking | inspect linked evidence, code, or analysis |
| RW-05 | Disposition | approve, revise, dispute, defer |
| RW-06 | State transition | update validation or review state |
| RW-07 | Audit logging | persist decision rationale |

## 8. Triage priorities

| Priority | Typical case |
| --- | --- |
| Critical | high-trust outputs, policy-code divergence, privileged objects |
| High | accepted analysis that may trigger maintenance, high-risk code |
| Medium | ordinary promotion from distilled to reviewed |
| Low | exploratory or low-impact research objects |

## 9. Approval criteria

### 9.1 Primary atoms

A primary atom SHOULD be approved only if:

| Criterion | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| provenance complete | yes |
| type assignment valid | yes |
| scope clear | yes |
| contradiction handled | yes |
| wording materially sound | yes |

### 9.2 Analytical atoms

An analytical object SHOULD be accepted only if:

| Criterion | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| analysis provenance complete | yes |
| source scope clear | yes |
| analytical/object boundary preserved | yes |
| finding useful and not contaminating canon | yes |
| review rationale recorded | yes |

### 9.3 Relations

A relation SHOULD be approved only if:

| Criterion | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| endpoints valid | yes |
| relation type semantically correct | yes |
| provenance or rationale present | yes |
| no unmarked contradiction introduced | yes |

## 10. Disposition outcomes

| Outcome | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| approve_primary | promote toward reviewed/verified |
| approve_analysis | promote toward reviewed/accepted |
| request_revision | return for refinement or clarification |
| dispute | mark disputed and exclude from normal assembly |
| defer | hold for future review |
| reject_relation | do not publish or remove pending edge |

## 11. Required audit record

Each review decision MUST record:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| review event ID | unique decision event |
| target ref | reviewed object |
| reviewer | actor |
| old state | prior state |
| new state | updated state |
| rationale | explanation |
| supporting refs | evidence/trace links |
| timestamp | when decided |

## 12. Review quality metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| average review turnaround | operational responsiveness |
| dispute rate | instability or ambiguity level |
| reversal rate | later overturns |
| accepted-to-disputed ratio | trust calibration health |
| missing-provenance rejection rate | intake/refinery weakness indicator |

## 13. Final normative judgment

A valid UAKS-v1 review workflow MUST record explicit dispositions, MUST preserve rationale and audit trails, MUST distinguish primary verification from analytical acceptance, and SHOULD prioritize high-consequence review items first.

# Remaining Steps to Phase Completion

## Current Position

You have already established most of the **foundational architecture-and-governance phase**. What is complete in draft form includes the core architecture, ontology, schema pack, text and code refinery specs, analysis integration lane, retrieval scoring, template catalog, provenance rendering, trace and impact formats, resolver contract, query patterns, access model, portal projection model, pilot intake SOP, and review workflow SOP.

That means the remaining work is no longer about inventing the system. It is about **closing the operational, evaluation, and launch-control gaps** so the phase can end in a coherent, pilot-launchable package.

* * *

## Phase Completion Definition

For this phase to count as complete, you need all of the following rings closed:

| Ring | Completion condition |
| --- | --- |
| Governance | rules exist and are mutually consistent |
| Architecture | topology, boundaries, and storage decisions are frozen for v1 |
| Schemas | object model is machine-oriented and stable enough for implementation |
| Pipelines | text, code, and analysis intake are normatively specified |
| Retrieval / Assembly | ranking, templates, reporting, and rendering rules are defined |
| Access / Workflow | auth, intake, review, and projection behavior are defined |
| Operations | change and incident control are defined |
| Evaluation | benchmark, gold set, harness, and readiness scoring are defined |

You are now mainly missing the last two rings.

* * *

# Operations Pack v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate (UAKS)

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_operations_pack_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Operational governance specification

This pack consolidates the operational control layer required for pilot deployment. It governs **change discipline, incident handling, daily operations, monitoring, and release control**.

The goal is to ensure the substrate remains **stable, auditable, and recoverable** once real data and workflows begin interacting with it.

* * *

# 1. Change Management SOP v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_change_management_sop_20260311`

## 1.1 Purpose

Change management governs modifications to:

*   ontology
    
*   schemas
    
*   refinery pipelines
    
*   relation catalog
    
*   templates
    
*   trust policies
    
*   operational workflows
    
*   codebase implementing the system
    

Without formal change control, atomic substrates quickly drift into inconsistent semantics.

* * *

## 1.2 Change categories

| Category | Examples | Risk level |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Schema change | atom fields, relation definitions | high |
| Ontology change | new atom family, new relation type | high |
| Pipeline change | new refinery logic, parser upgrade | medium-high |
| Retrieval change | scoring weights or query behavior | medium |
| Template change | rendering structures | medium |
| Workflow change | review policy adjustments | medium |
| Operational change | deployment scripts, environment config | low-medium |

* * *

## 1.3 Change request structure

Every change MUST have a **Change Request (CR)**.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `changeId` | unique identifier |
| `changeType` | schema, ontology, pipeline, etc |
| `summary` | short description |
| `rationale` | why the change exists |
| `impactScope` | affected components |
| `riskLevel` | low / medium / high |
| `rollbackStrategy` | how to revert |
| `testPlan` | verification procedure |
| `submittedBy` | actor |
| `timestamp` | submission time |

* * *

## 1.4 Change workflow

| Phase | Description |
| --- | --- |
| CR-01 | Change request submitted |
| CR-02 | Triage and classification |
| CR-03 | Technical review |
| CR-04 | Risk evaluation |
| CR-05 | Implementation |
| CR-06 | Validation testing |
| CR-07 | Deployment |
| CR-08 | Post-change monitoring |

* * *

## 1.5 Change approval rules

| Change type | Minimum approval |
| --- | --- |
| ontology | administrator + reviewer |
| schema | administrator + operator |
| refinery | operator + reviewer |
| retrieval | reviewer |
| template | reviewer |
| workflow | administrator |
| operational | operator |

* * *

## 1.6 Rollback requirement

All changes MUST have a rollback plan.

Typical rollback methods:

| Method | Example |
| --- | --- |
| supersession reversal | restore prior schema version |
| pipeline revert | redeploy previous refinery build |
| recipe reset | revert assembly behavior |
| projection rebuild | regenerate projections |

* * *

# 2. Incident Response SOP v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_incident_response_sop_20260311`

## 2.1 Purpose

This procedure governs response to system incidents affecting **data integrity, trust state, system availability, or operational correctness**.

* * *

## 2.2 Incident categories

| Category | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Data integrity incident | corrupted or incorrect atoms |
| Trust incident | incorrect validation state or promotion |
| Pipeline incident | refinery failure or parser regression |
| Retrieval incident | incorrect ranking behavior |
| Projection incident | broken workspace views |
| Security incident | unauthorized access or mutation |
| Performance incident | degraded system responsiveness |

* * *

## 2.3 Incident severity

| Level | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| S1 | critical integrity or security failure |
| S2 | major operational disruption |
| S3 | moderate issue affecting subset of users |
| S4 | minor anomaly |

* * *

## 2.4 Incident response workflow

| Phase | Description |
| --- | --- |
| IR-01 | detection |
| IR-02 | classification |
| IR-03 | containment |
| IR-04 | diagnosis |
| IR-05 | remediation |
| IR-06 | verification |
| IR-07 | incident report |

* * *

## 2.5 Example incidents

### Pipeline parser failure

Symptoms:

*   refinery stops producing atoms
    
*   AST parse errors spike
    

Response:

1.  pause pipeline
    
2.  revert parser build
    
3.  reprocess failed batch
    
4.  generate incident report
    

* * *

### Incorrect trust promotion

Symptoms:

*   machine-derived analysis marked accepted
    

Response:

1.  mark affected atoms disputed
    
2.  audit review events
    
3.  correct validation state
    
4.  patch review logic
    

* * *

# 3. Runbook Set v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_runbook_set_20260311`

Runbooks provide practical operational procedures.

* * *

2.  run refinery pipelines
    
3.  monitor parse success rate
    
4.  inspect anomaly logs
    
5.  verify atom publication counts

Steps:

1.  validate analysis envelope
    
2.  run normalization membrane
    
3.  publish analysis objects
    
4.  check review queue
    

* * *

## 3.3 Projection rebuild runbook

Steps:

1.  invalidate stale projection cache
    
2.  regenerate projection nodes
    
3.  verify state hash
    
4.  update portal view
    

* * *

## 3.4 Retrieval tuning runbook

Steps:

1.  run evaluation harness
    
2.  inspect ranking anomalies
    
3.  adjust weights
    
4.  re-evaluate benchmark queries
    

* * *

Observability ensures operational visibility.

# Evaluation Pack A v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate (UAKS)

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_evaluation_pack_A_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Evaluation specification

This pack defines the **evaluation inputs** required to measure whether the architecture behaves correctly.

It establishes:

*   the **Benchmark Corpus**
    
*   the **Gold-Standard Atom Set**
    
*   canonical **evaluation queries**
    
*   baseline **trace expectations**
    

Without these artifacts the system cannot be objectively evaluated.

* * *

# 1. Benchmark Corpus Definition v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_benchmark_corpus_definition_20260311`

## 1.1 Purpose

The benchmark corpus is a controlled dataset used to test:

*   text refinery correctness
    
*   code refinery correctness
    
*   atomization behavior
    
*   deduplication behavior
    
*   relation extraction
    
*   retrieval ranking
    
*   trace accuracy
    

It must represent the **variety of real-world inputs** the system will face.

* * *

## 1.2 Corpus design principles

| Principle | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| coverage | represent multiple source types |
| complexity | include both simple and multi-claim sources |
| ambiguity | include ambiguous cases to test refinery discipline |
| scale | large enough to test retrieval |
| traceability | every source must have expected atom outputs |

* * *

## 1.3 Corpus categories

The benchmark corpus SHOULD include these source families.

| Category | Examples |
| --- | --- |
| conversational | transcripts, meeting notes |
| documentation | specifications, memos |
| policy | rules and normative statements |
| technical | code repositories and scripts |
| configuration | YAML, JSON, schema definitions |
| analytical | external analysis bundles |

* * *

## 1.4 Minimum corpus composition

| Source type | Minimum count |
| --- | --- |
| textual documents | 100 |
| policy documents | 20 |
| code repositories | 10 |
| code files | 500 |
| configuration files | 50 |
| analysis bundles | 30 |

This scale allows meaningful retrieval testing.

* * *

## 1.5 Corpus structure

Each corpus item MUST contain metadata.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `sourceId` | unique identifier |
| `sourceType` | document, code, config, analysis |
| `origin` | where it came from |
| `scope` | project/domain |
| `timestamp` | when captured |
| `expectedAtoms` | approximate expected yield |
| `notes` | special features |

* * *

## 1.6 Corpus storage

The benchmark corpus SHOULD be stored as:

*   immutable CAS objects
    
*   registered SourceObjects
    
*   reproducible dataset snapshot
    

* * *

# 2. Gold-Standard Atom Set Specification v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_gold_atom_set_spec_20260311`

## 2.1 Purpose

The gold-standard atom set defines the **correct atomization output** for benchmark sources.

It is used to evaluate:

*   refinery correctness
    
*   atomic boundary decisions
    
*   relation extraction
    
*   deduplication behavior
    

* * *

## 2.2 Gold atom design rules

Each gold atom MUST:

*   represent a **single independent claim or function**
    
*   include **exact source span**
    
*   include **correct atom type**
    
*   include **expected relations**
    

* * *

## 2.3 Gold atom schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `goldAtomId` | canonical atom identifier |
| `sourceRef` | original source |
| `atomType` | TextAtom / PolicyAtom / CodeAtom / EvidenceAtom |
| `canonicalStatement` | distilled meaning |
| `sourceSpan` | exact location |
| `expectedRelations` | relation edges |
| `validationState` | expected trust level |

* * *

## 2.4 Gold relation specification

Relations in the gold set define expected graph structure.

| Relation | Example |
| --- | --- |
| `SUPPORTED_BY` | claim supported by evidence |
| `IMPLEMENTS` | code implements rule |
| `AFFECTS` | policy affects code |
| `DERIVED_FROM` | atom from source |

* * *

## 2.5 Example gold atom

```
goldAtomId: gold_atom_014sourceRef: src_policy_002atomType: PolicyAtomcanonicalStatement: Imported analysis bundles must pass normalization before publication.sourceSpan: lines 12–15expectedRelations:  - AFFECTS: atom_code_import_pipelinevalidationState: VERIFIED
```

* * *

# 3. Evaluation Query Dataset v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_query_eval_dataset_20260311`

## 3.1 Purpose

The query dataset evaluates whether retrieval produces the correct objects.

Queries simulate real use.

* * *

## 3.2 Query categories

| Category | Example |
| --- | --- |
| concept query | “analysis normalization rules” |
| code query | “function that parses analysis bundles” |
| policy query | “rules for accepting analysis findings” |
| trace query | “what code implements the validation policy” |
| impact query | “what changes if normalization rule changes” |
| research query | “analysis techniques used in corpus” |

* * *

## 3.3 Dataset size

| Query type | Minimum count |
| --- | --- |
| concept queries | 100 |
| code queries | 100 |
| trace queries | 50 |
| impact queries | 50 |
| research queries | 50 |

Total minimum: **350 queries**

* * *

## 3.4 Query schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `queryId` | unique identifier |
| `queryText` | natural language query |
| `intentClass` | concept/code/policy/etc |
| `expectedObjects` | gold results |
| `acceptableAlternatives` | secondary acceptable answers |

* * *

# 4. Trace Benchmark Specification v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_trace_benchmark_spec_20260311`

## 4.1 Purpose

This benchmark tests graph traversal accuracy.

* * *

## 4.2 Trace scenarios

| Scenario | Test |
| --- | --- |
| rule-to-code | policy implemented by code |
| claim-to-evidence | claim supported by sources |
| change impact | dependency graph correctness |
| analysis-to-review | drift triggers review |

* * *

## 4.3 Example scenario

```
Origin: policy_atom_005Expected path:policy_atom_005  -> AFFECTScode_atom_validation  -> TESTScode_atom_validation_tests
```

* * *

## 4.4 Evaluation criteria

| Criterion | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| path correctness | graph path matches gold set |
| missing edges | missing relation detection |
| extra edges | false positives |
| trace completeness | full path coverage |

* * *

# 5. Evaluation Dataset Packaging

The evaluation pack MUST include:

| Artifact | Description |
| --- | --- |
| corpus snapshot | immutable dataset |
| gold atom dataset | expected outputs |
| gold relation dataset | expected graph |
| query dataset | evaluation queries |
| trace scenarios | graph traversal tests |

* * *

# 6. Evaluation Execution Rules

The evaluation harness MUST:

1.  ingest benchmark corpus
    
2.  run refinery pipelines
    
3.  compare produced atoms to gold set
    
4.  run retrieval queries
    
5.  compute retrieval metrics
    
6.  run trace scenarios
    
7.  produce evaluation report
    

* * *

# 7. Evaluation Metrics

## Refinery metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| atom precision | correct atoms produced |
| atom recall | expected atoms captured |
| relation precision | correct relations extracted |
| dedup accuracy | duplicate collapse correctness |

* * *

## Retrieval metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| precision@k | correct objects in top results |
| recall@k | coverage of gold results |
| MRR | ranking quality |
| semantic recall | conceptual retrieval ability |

* * *

## Trace metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| path accuracy | correct graph traversal |
| completeness | full path found |
| error rate | incorrect edges |

* * *

# Truth Lifecycle Pack v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate (UAKS)

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_truth_lifecycle_pack_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Governance specification

This pack defines how knowledge evolves inside the substrate without corrupting lineage or stability. It formalizes three pillars:

| Pillar | Function |
| --- | --- |
| Supersession | controlled replacement of atoms |
| Contradiction | coexistence and resolution of conflicting claims |
| Provenance | traceability from rendered output to raw source |

The objective is that **knowledge can change without losing history or traceability**.

* * *

# 1 Supersession Policy v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_supersession_policy_20260311`

## 1.1 Purpose

Supersession defines how atoms are replaced when knowledge evolves.

Atoms are **never overwritten**. Instead a new atom replaces the old one via a relation.

```
old_atom  →  IS_SUPERSEDED_BY  →  new_atom
```

This preserves the complete knowledge history.

* * *

## 1.2 Supersession triggers

Supersession occurs when:

| Trigger | Example |
| --- | --- |
| policy revision | rule updated |
| factual correction | incorrect statement corrected |
| analysis refinement | improved analytical interpretation |
| code revision | function implementation changed |
| ontology revision | classification corrected |

* * *

## 1.3 Supersession requirements

A valid supersession requires:

| Requirement | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| new atom created | old atom preserved |
| relation recorded | explicit supersession edge |
| provenance recorded | reason for replacement |
| timestamp | time of change |
| reviewer validation | if high-impact |

* * *

## 1.4 Supersession chain

Supersession chains form temporal lineage.

```
Atom A (2024)   ↓ superseded_byAtom B (2025)   ↓ superseded_byAtom C (2026)
```

Assemblies default to **latest accepted atom**.

Historical views may access older atoms.

* * *

## 1.5 Supersession safety rules

Assemblies MUST NOT:

*   mix superseded atoms with current atoms
    
*   hide supersession chains
    
*   discard historical atoms
    

* * *

# 2 Contradiction Handling Policy v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_contradiction_policy_20260311`

## 2.1 Purpose

Contradictions are expected in evolving knowledge systems.

Rather than deleting one claim, the system records the conflict.

```
atom_A  →  CONTRADICTS  →  atom_B
```

* * *

## 2.2 Types of contradiction

| Type | Example |
| --- | --- |
| factual contradiction | two conflicting statements |
| rule contradiction | incompatible policy rules |
| analytical disagreement | competing interpretations |
| evidence conflict | evidence undermines claim |

* * *

## 2.3 Contradiction states

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| detected | system identified potential conflict |
| under_review | human review required |
| accepted_conflict | competing interpretations allowed |
| resolved | one claim superseded |
| archived | conflict no longer relevant |

* * *

## 2.4 Conflict resolution paths

Conflicts can resolve via:

| Resolution | Description |
| --- | --- |
| supersession | one atom replaced |
| scope separation | both claims valid in different contexts |
| evidence expansion | new evidence clarifies |
| ontology change | classification corrected |

* * *

## 2.5 Assembly behavior

Assemblies must handle contradictions explicitly.

Possible behaviors:

| Mode | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| conservative | exclude disputed atoms |
| balanced | present both claims |
| exploratory | include conflicts and analysis |

* * *

# 3 Provenance Policy v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_provenance_policy_20260311`

## 3.1 Purpose

Every atom must be traceable back to its origin.

Without provenance the substrate becomes untrustworthy.

* * *

## 3.2 Provenance layers

Three layers are required.

| Layer | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| source provenance | original source material |
| transformation provenance | refinery operations |
| analysis provenance | analytical interpretations |

* * *

## 3.3 Provenance schema

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| sourceObjectId | original source |
| sourceSpan | exact location |
| extractionMethod | refinery method |
| transformationChain | processing steps |
| reviewerId | human validation |
| timestamp | creation time |

* * *

## 3.4 Provenance example

```
atomId: atom_policy_021sourceObjectId: src_doc_034sourceSpan: paragraph_12extractionMethod: semantic_refinery_v2reviewerId: reviewer_002timestamp: 2026-03-11
```

* * *

## 3.5 Provenance guarantees

The system must guarantee:

| Guarantee | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| lineage traceability | every atom linked to source |
| transformation transparency | refinery steps recorded |
| auditability | reviewers identifiable |
| reproducibility | atom derivation reproducible |

* * *

# 4 Temporal Validity Model

Atoms exist within temporal context.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| validFrom | first valid time |
| validUntil | supersession or expiry |
| supersededBy | replacement atom |

This allows assemblies to render **historical knowledge views**.

* * *

# 5 Trust State Model

Trust states prevent premature canonization of machine outputs.

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| imported | raw system output |
| machine_derived | produced by analysis |
| reviewed | human examined |
| accepted | allowed in normal assemblies |
| disputed | challenged |
| deprecated | superseded |

Assemblies default to **accepted objects only**.

* * *

# 6 Lifecycle Flow

The lifecycle of an atom follows this path.

```
Source   ↓Refinery   ↓Atom creation   ↓Validation state   ↓Accepted substrate   ↓Supersession or contradiction   ↓Historical archive
```

* * *

# 7 Lifecycle Integrity Rules

The system must enforce:

| Rule | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| atoms immutable | no in-place edits |
| supersession explicit | replacement always linked |
| contradictions recorded | conflicts preserved |
| provenance mandatory | origin always traceable |

* * *

# Truth Lifecycle Pack Completion

This pack ensures the substrate can:

*   evolve knowledge
    
*   preserve history
    
*   manage contradictions
    
*   maintain traceability
    

Without these rules the atomic system would quickly degrade.

* * *

| **Final** | Launch Pack | capacity model and readiness scoring |

These final two packs close the **system integrity and launch readiness layer**.

## A: 
# Integrity Pack v1

## Unified Atomic Knowledge Substrate (UAKS)

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_integrity_pack_20260311`  
**Version:** `1.0-draft`  
**Status:** Technical integrity specification

This pack defines the **technical guarantees that prevent corruption, duplication, or semantic drift of atomic objects**.

It covers four integrity pillars:

| Pillar | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Content Addressing | immutable identity for all atoms |
| Canonicalization | deterministic hashing |
| Atomicity Testing | validation of refinery output |
| Deduplication | prevention of semantic duplication |

These rules ensure the atomic substrate behaves as a **stable, verifiable knowledge ledger**.

* * *

# 1 Hashing Standard v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_hashing_standard_20260311`

## 1.1 Purpose

Every atomic object must have a **content-derived identifier**.

The identifier is a cryptographic fingerprint of the atom’s canonical form.

This guarantees:

*   immutability
    
*   reproducibility
    
*   deduplication
    

* * *

## 1.2 Hash algorithm

The system uses:

```
SHA-256
```

Reasons:

| Property | Benefit |
| --- | --- |
| deterministic | same input produces same ID |
| collision resistance | prevents identity conflicts |
| industry standard | widely supported |

* * *

## 1.3 Atom identifier structure

```
atom:<hash>
```

Example

```
atom:7fa9c1c9c8d23a...
```

* * *

## 1.4 Hash input specification

The hash MUST be calculated from the atom’s **canonical payload**.

Canonical payload includes:

| Field | Included |
| --- | --- |
| canonical statement | yes |
| atom type | yes |
| normalized metadata | yes |
| source reference | yes |
| whitespace | normalized |

Excluded:

| Field | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| timestamps | would change identity |
| validation state | mutable |
| reviewer ID | mutable |

* * *

# 2 Canonicalization Specification v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_canonicalization_spec_20260311`

## 2.1 Purpose

Canonicalization ensures that semantically identical atoms produce identical hashes.

Without canonicalization the system would accumulate duplicates.

* * *

## 2.2 Canonicalization rules

| Rule | Description |
| --- | --- |
| whitespace normalization | collapse extra spaces |
| punctuation normalization | consistent punctuation |
| case normalization | lowercase where appropriate |
| field ordering | deterministic field order |
| metadata normalization | stable schema order |

* * *

## 2.3 Canonical payload format

Example canonical representation:

```
{  "atomType": "PolicyAtom",  "canonicalStatement": "analysis bundles must pass normalization before publication",  "sourceObjectId": "src_doc_034",  "sourceSpan": "p12"}
```

Hash is computed over this structure.

* * *

# 3 Atomicity Test Suite v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_atomicity_test_suite_20260311`

## 3.1 Purpose

Ensures refinery output respects atomic boundaries.

An atom must represent the **smallest independent unit of meaning**.

* * *

## 3.2 Atomicity rules

| Rule | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| independence | atom understandable alone |
| single claim | only one factual or logical claim |
| minimal scope | no compound assertions |
| stable reference | clear provenance |

* * *

## 3.3 Atomicity test cases

### Test 1: compound claim detection

Example:

```
Normalization must occur before ingestion and the pipeline must log errors.
```

Expected result:

Two atoms:

```
Normalization must occur before ingestion.Pipeline must log errors.
```

* * *

### Test 2: dependency leakage

Invalid atom:

```
The system must run the validation module defined earlier.
```

Reason: reference unclear.

Expected refinement:

```
The validation module must run before ingestion.
```

* * *

### Test 3: ambiguous entity detection

Invalid:

```
The system must check it before publishing.
```

Expected:

```
The ingestion system must validate analysis bundles before publishing them.
```

* * *

# 4 Deduplication Protocol v1

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_deduplication_protocol_20260311`

## 4.1 Purpose

Prevents storage of redundant atoms.

* * *

## 4.2 Deduplication methods

| Method | Description |
| --- | --- |
| hash equality | identical canonical payload |
| semantic similarity | embedding proximity |
| relation overlap | identical relation structures |

* * *

## 4.3 Deduplication workflow

| Step | Description |
| --- | --- |
| D-01 | canonicalize atom |
| D-02 | compute hash |
| D-03 | check CAS store |
| D-04 | check semantic similarity |
| D-05 | merge or store |

* * *

## 4.4 Merge policy

If duplicate detected:

| Scenario | Action |
| --- | --- |
| identical content | reuse atom |
| near duplicate | merge provenance |
| conflicting statement | create contradiction relation |

* * *

# 5 Integrity Verification Procedure

**Document ID:** `uaks_v1_integrity_verification_procedure_20260311`

Periodic integrity checks must verify:

| Check | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| hash validation | payload matches stored hash |
| CAS consistency | no missing blobs |
| graph integrity | valid relation endpoints |
| provenance completeness | all atoms traceable |

* * *

## Verification schedule

| Frequency | Task |
| --- | --- |
| hourly | ingestion integrity checks |
| daily | CAS consistency scan |
| weekly | graph integrity scan |
| monthly | full substrate audit |

* * *

# 6 Integrity Failure Handling

If corruption occurs:

| Step | Action |
| --- | --- |
| detect | anomaly alert |
| isolate | quarantine affected atoms |
| reconstruct | regenerate from source |
| audit | trace cause |
| restore | reintroduce verified atoms |

* * *

# Integrity Pack Completion

With this pack the system guarantees:

*   immutable atom identity
    
*   deterministic hashing
    
*   refinery atomic discipline
    
*   duplicate prevention
    
*   substrate integrity verification
    

These guarantees are essential for large-scale atomic systems.

| conversation transcript | 5–20 atoms per paragraph |
| documentation | 3–10 atoms per paragraph |
| policy document | 2–6 atoms per paragraph |
| code file | 10–40 code atoms |

| EvidenceAtoms | 30,000 |
| AnalysisAtoms | 15,000 |

~255,000 atoms

~400,000–700,000 graph edges

20–50 GB

## 1. Automated Repository Generator Script

### One-command local scaffold for the entire system

Save this as `scripts/bootstrap_uaks_repo.sh`, then run it with environment variables.

Bash
